TP4: k=3 accepts 53% vs 30% at k=7 (prose 31–33 vs 26–28 tok/s; k=2 and 5 are worse; without speculation 22.6). | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `DFLASH_TOKENS / num_speculative_tokens` | 7 (trained block of 8); TP4: 3 | Number of tokens drafted per step (num_speculative_tokens in --speculative-config). | On TP4, 3 tokens = 53% accepted vs 30% with 7 (prose 31–33 vs 26–28 tok/s); it also sizes buffers (MAX_NUM_SEQS × (k+1)). | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `draft_sample_method` | probabilistic | The drafter samples its tokens probabilistically instead of greedily. · Drafts are sampled from the drafter's distribution rather than chosen greedily. | Combines with rejection sampling to preserve the target distribution at temperature &gt; 0. · Combined with the draft_logits written by the selector, it enables lossless probabilistic verification. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K` | off (default); ema/on/1 · off; ema / on / 1 | Verifies only a prefix (2, 4 or 7) of the 7 drafts per step, guided by an EMA of the acceptance. · Verifies only a prefix (2, 4 or 7) of the 7 drafts, chosen by an EMA of the acceptance and uniform across the batch. | Lossless at temperature 0; prose +13–21% (Silk Road +21%, sky/sunset +13%, hash-map +10%, code +5–15%). Uses the minimum across requests because FLASHINFER_MLA_SPARSE_SM120 and KDA only support uniform FULL graphs. · Prose +13–21% (2026-09-08) with no loss at temperature 0; keeps the batch uniform, as FULL CUDA graphs require. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_ALPHA` | 0.25 | α of the exponential moving average of the per-request acceptance. · Weight of the per-request acceptance EMA. | A larger α reacts faster and oscillates more. · Sets how quickly the chosen k reacts to changes in acceptance. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_FILE` | /root/.cache/vllm/glm53_adaptive_k.json (host ~/.cache/vllm-glm53-flash/glm53_adaptive_k.json) | Override file read at runtime. · Runtime override file, reread every 50 steps if its mtime changes. | Retune without a reboot; {"mode":"off"} goes back to k=7. · Retuning without a reboot; the set is clipped to the boot set because graphs only exist for it. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_HIST` | 200 | Size of the adaptive-k decision histogram. · Logs the histogram of the chosen k every N steps. | Used to observe and diagnose the policy. · Observability of the adaptive-k behavior. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_MARGIN` | 1.0 | n = largest value in the set ≤ ceil(ema + margin). · Optimistic margin: n = largest value in the set ≤ ceil(ema + margin). | The margin tilts the choice toward verifying more drafts. · A larger margin verifies more drafts (more chances to accept) at the cost of extra rows in the step. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_MIN_STEPS` | 4 | Steps observed before cutting k. · Steps observed at full length before starting to cut k. | Avoids reducing k based on few samples. · Warmup: avoids cutting k without enough statistics. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_SATURATE` | max · max; n | Value observed when all verified drafts were accepted. · If all drafts are accepted, feeds the EMA with the maximum (it can go up) or with n (it can only go down). | If the verifier only looked at 2 and accepted both, the real acceptance may be higher; saturating at max lets k go up again. · Avoids the ratchet effect (anti-ratchet) in which k can only decrease. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `GLM53_ADAPTIVE_K_SET` | 2,4,7; experiment 2,3,4,5,7 | Candidate lengths; one graph is captured for each k+1. · Candidate verification lengths; graphs are captured for k+1 ([3,5,8]). | Requires capture sizes that are multiples of 3, 5 and 8 (computed automatically on TP2). · The capture sizes must include multiples of 3, 5 and 8. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `kv_cache_dtype (speculative-config)` | auto (BF16; 2048 B/token/layer at the TP2 boot) | Dtype of the drafter's KV. | 'The dense DFlash2 cannot use the target's fp8_ds_mla layout and SM121 has no FA3/FA4 for plain FP8'. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 23 Quantized KV cache |
| `MTP_TOKENS` | 2 | num_speculative_tokens of the native MTP (MTP block at layer 45). · k of the native MTP when SPEC_METHOD=mtp. | It is the DFlash2 rollback; the MTP capture sizes are 1 2 3 4 6 8 12. · Baseline of ~24.6 tok/s on prose; a safe rollback from DFlash2. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `rejection_sample_method` | standard | Standard rejection-sampling verification. · Uses standard rejection sampling to verify the drafts. | Guarantees lossless speculation: the output follows the target distribution. · Guarantees output with the same distribution as the target (accepts with prob. min(1, p/q) and resamples from the residual). | 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `SPEC_METHOD` | dflash (.env.example:133); mtp; none | Picks the speculative decoding method that the launcher sets up. | The rollback is SPEC_METHOD=mtp (k=2, ~24.6 tok/s on prose); with dflash, structured reaches 62.9–65.1 tok/s. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |

## GLM-5.3-Flash EXL3 (TensorFold)

### Modifications (patches, overlays, kernels, hotfixes, checkpoint transforms)

#### API / serving

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0037-cuda-tokenize` | no setting | /tokenize and /detokenize routes (also under /v1/) with vLLM's fields: prompt (with add_special_tokens) or rendered messages → {count, max_model_len, tokens}; /v1/completions now accepts a prompt as a list of ids. | Clients written for vLLM use these routes to count tokens on the server, after the chat template. | 34 API, tools & JSON, 05 Tokens & embeddings |
| `0044-cuda-context-errors` | no setting | Context errors in OpenAI's format: HTTP 400 with "code": "context_length_exceeded" and param (messages or prompt); the GLM app's window refusal and prompts with images past the window take the same path (503 for CapacityError). | Agent clients that compact the conversation when they get this error now work. | 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `0084-anthropic-messages` | v1.9; /v1/messages, /messages, …/count_tokens | A backport of TensorFold v0.6.3's Anthropic Messages API: /v1/messages (and /messages, with /count_tokens) translated to the same chat completion the OpenAI route serves: system, text and image blocks, tool_use / tool_result, thinking as reasoning_content, stop_sequences, output_ | Claude Code and the Anthropic SDKs talk to the server directly, with no translating proxy; v1.3.2 only had OpenAI routes. The recipe sets no API key: expose it only on a trusted network or behind a gateway (interpretation). | 34 API, tools & JSON, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |

#### Attention / kernels

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0009-glm-prefill-kernels` | TF_GLM_EXL3_ROWROT=1, TF_GLM_SPARSE_ONEPASS=1, TF_GLM_SCORE_RB=4 | Prefill waste cuts: the EXL3 input rotation once per row (a suh shared by all experts); dense attention only on rows before SPARSE_FROM = 2051; sparse attention in one online-softmax pass, without partials; scoring only the visible pools (a multiple of 1,024) and a 2,051-token li | No isolated number: the split's gain (0010) was measured with 0009 and 0020. Same bits, except one-pass (fixed bits per row, other than sparse_attention's). | 13 Sparse attention, 28 Kernels |
| `0012-glm-kda-chunked` | TF_GLM_KDA_CHUNKED (0 → 1 via KDA_CHUNKED); Triton knobs _PREC/_BV/_STATE_ROWS/_KSPLIT (removed in 0039) | Prefill of the 34 KDA layers in chunked (WY/UT) form, in 5 Triton kernels: 64-row sub-chunks at absolute positions; T = (I + L)^-1 by forward substitution in 16-row blocks; W = T(β·k·e^G), U = T(β·v), V' = U − W S0ᵀ, O = (q·e^G) S0ᵀ + M V'; only the state pass is sequential. | In v0.6.0 the KDA prefill ran the recurrence row by row. Exponents are formed around a reference point per 16-row block so they never exceed e^75 (&lt; fp32's e^88.7). With 0014 + 0039: 50k 29.3 → 26.4 s; 149k 91.1 → 84.5 s. Close to serial, not the same bits. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `0014-glm-kda-chunked-gb10` | constants (LAUNCH, GROUP) | Stability and parity of the Triton chunked KDA: each exponent is summed outward from the reference (never as a difference of G's); kernels with enable_fp_fusion=False, libdevice.exp, div_rn and sqrt_rn to match kda.cu (compiled with --fmad=false); a single pipeline stage. | G reaches −320 within a sub-chunk and ulp(320) ≈ 3e-5 leaked into decays near 1 (the first version had 10× the error of kda.cu's chain). GB10 (sm_121) gives a block 99 KiB of shared memory (101,376 B) and the pipelined loads needed up to 154 KiB. Read-outs ~3e-7 relative to float64. | 04 Numbers in the computer, 26 Inside the GPU |
| `0024-glm-prompt-select-rows` | constant SELECT_ROWS = 512 | Prompt token selection (_select_prompt) processes rows in blocks of 512: each block scores the same pools, selects the top-512 and writes its slice; the score buffer becomes 512 × pool_bucket instead of R × pool_bucket. | Less memory at 1M: with R = 2,048 and ~262,144 visible pools, ~2 GiB against ~512 MiB [estimate]. The same columns, so the same bits per row. | 13 Sparse attention, 27 Unified memory |
| `0026-glm-multi-kda` | no knob; used with PARALLEL > 1 | KDA for several sequences in one window: a segment table int32 [nseg, 6] (first row, rows, state slot, parity, conv slot, keep); segment i runs from rec[slot, parity] to rec[slot, 1 − parity]; replay_layers_seg_kernel redoes the first keep rows when only some were accepted. | To verify several requests' windows in one forward, each KDA layer must start from each sequence's state; a segment's bits equal a solo run of its rows. Zero-row segments are skipped, so a table sized for the most streams serves fewer in a graph. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 32 Scheduler & batching |
| `0029-glm-multi-dsa` | PARALLEL > 1 (constants SEG_SPLIT, SEG_SCORE_GRID = 256, SEG_SELECT) | Segmented DSA windows: device-side SegRows (per row pos, base, seg, sparse; per segment start, rows, pool base and pools seen); segmented kernels for latent writes, scoring and selection; top-512 pools by an 8-bit radix select (4 passes), ties to the lower pool; split selection f | Each stream has its latent and indexer keys in its own extent; the kernels reproduce the solo tiles, and integer atomics give the same result in any order. One graph per window size covers any mix of positions. | 13 Sparse attention, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0031-glm-decode-index-regs` | always on | _scores_rows: in decode, one program per (row, g) loads the index query [H, 128] and the per-head weights once into registers and walks the pool blocks grid-stride (128 programs per row), in a single launch for all rows. | Before, each program reloaded the query for every 64-pool block, in launches per row block. The same tile (64 pools, 4 warps), so the same bits; 8 warps or 32-pool blocks would give others. No isolated number. | 13 Sparse attention, 28 Kernels |
| `0039-glm-kda-chunked-kernel` | KDA_CHUNKED=1 → TF_GLM_KDA_CHUNKED=1; 64 grid | Chunked KDA in a single CUDA kernel: one 256-thread block per head walks 32-row sub-chunks with the 128×128 fp32 state in registers; decays as running products (never e^(+x) nor a difference of G's); compiled without FMA contraction, with CUDA's expf and IEEE division (the fast d | The Triton version (5 kernels, 64-row sub-chunks) kept intermediates in global memory and had G down to −320 (with 32 rows, −160). A row's bits depend only on its sub-chunk and input state, hence the 64 grid. With 0012 + 0014: 50k 29.3 → 26.4 s; 149k 91.1 → 84.5 s. Removes the Triton knobs TF_GLM_KDA_PREC/BV/STATE_ROWS/KSPLIT. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `0043-glm-visible-pools` | always on | _visible_bound = min(NPS, max((P + r + 1)//4, K)): a decode row's scoring and selection (per row and split) only scan the complete pools it can see, inside the graph's bucket. | Sparse graphs are captured per power-of-two bucket (exact sizes fragmented memory at 128k) and scanned every column, although the extra ones are −∞. The same tokens (no speed number); extends TensorFold's PR #140. | 13 Sparse attention, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0085-glm-select-split-loop` | v1.9; by BadAd84; the same tokens | The last pass of the DSA indexer's split top-k selection (select_split) summed the whole chunk table of the selection scratch, which is sized for the pool (512 chunks a row at PARALLEL=4 on 3 Sparks), not for the row's context. It now sums only the earlier chunks' histograms, 16  | The whole selection call at 16 rows: 0.96 → 0.10 ms at 35k, 5.79 → 0.28 at 226k, 15.68 → 0.64 at 590k, 27.87 → 1.08 at 1.04M (11 layers a round). Live on 3 Sparks, PARALLEL=4, one request: decode at 35k / 226k / 590k 182.6 / 111.2 / 66.2 → 203.0 / 191.0 / 184.4 tok/s, replies byte-identical (integer sums in another order). General lesson: | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 13 Sparse attention |

#### Distributed / network

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0006-cuda-roce-allgather` | TF_GLM_COMM=roce (COMM), TF_ROCE_MAX_KB=512, TF_ROCE_HCA, TF_ROCE_GID_INDEX | One-shot all-gather by RDMA WRITE (b12x's RoCEnante transport): in one kernel call each block copies its share into a pinned host region; the last block rings the doorbell; a C proxy thread (libibverbs) RDMA-writes each HCA stripe followed by a 4-byte seq flag on the same QP; thr | A decode round's all-gathers are small and NCCL spends most of each in fixed latency (~30–50 µs). On GB10 pinned host memory is GPU-addressable at full bandwidth and the NIC registers it with ibv_reg_mr (there is no GPUDirect RDMA). 11 µs vs 45 µs per 16 KiB; decode +6%. Same bits. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0010-glm-hc-split` | TF_GLM_HC_SPLIT (0 → 1 via SPLIT), TF_GLM_PREFILL_OVERLAP, TF_GLM_OVERLAP_PIECES=2, TF_GLM_HC_SPLIT_MIN_ROWS=64, TF_GLM_HC_EXCHANGE=p2p | The hyper-connection glue (24 products over 16,384 columns + Sinkhorn per row) split by rows between the ranks in prefill: each rank sends the peer the fp32 partial of the peer's rows, sums its own in rank order, runs hc_post + hc_pre on them and swaps the normalized bf16 rows; t | Without the split, both ranks ran the same glue on every row. Traffic per row goes from 4 B·D to 3 B·D and the glue halves [estimate]. 50k ~1,270 → ~1,730 tok/s (with 0009 and 0020); decode −~1.5%. Same bits. | 38 Parallelism, 15 Hyper-connections |
| `0017-glm-overlap-priority` | TF_GLM_OVERLAP_PRIORITY (−1 in this patch) | Makes the priority of the split overlap's side stream configurable (before, always high, priority=-1). | A high-priority stream in the process can cost decode rounds a little (graphs on the default stream), measured per boot; 0022 later changes the default to normal. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup, 45 Benchmarking |
| `0022-glm-overlap-normal-priority` | TF_GLM_OVERLAP_PRIORITY: −1 → 0 | Reverses 0017's default: the overlap's side stream now has normal priority; only −1 asks for high. | On two Sparks, two boots each: prefill the same and decode after a high-priority prompt ~0.5–1% slower. A feature that only helps prefill should not charge decode. | 45 Benchmarking, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0033-glm-prefill-overlap2` | SPLIT=1 → TF_GLM_PREFILL_OVERLAP=2 | During the HC glue's row exchanges, the main stream runs the next block's front on its own rows (kda_front, dsa_front, mlp_front, shared_front); the block then runs the front only on the peer's rows (undone). | The main stream waited for the last piece's exchange with nothing to do. A 149k prompt 92.8 → 90.7 s (with COPY_CODE, two boots each); same bits, because the prompt matmul gives the same bits for any row slicing. | 15 Hyper-connections, 38 Parallelism |
| `0040-glm-parallel-deadlocks` | TF_GLM_MULTI_WATCHDOG_S=300 | Fixes hangs: a gather's RoCE × NCCL choice now depends only on its byte count; dedicated buffers for the messages; the startup profile runs on both ranks; the scheduler thread catches exceptions and fails the live requests; a watchdog dumps the Python stacks. | The choice depended on the address alignment (data_ptr % 16): one rank could wait on RoCE and the other on NCCL forever. Collectives must match in operation, order and transport. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0052-cuda-roce-startup` | TF_ROCE_WAIT_S=20 (1–3600), COMM=roce | Until the engine is built, each eager RoCE all-gather waits for the peer in an NCCL barrier; the RoCE kernel builds during setup; the wait becomes configurable; failures print the proxy's counters (doorbell, posted, completed, per HCA). | Fixes #6: on a first start each rank builds its extensions alone and they could drift tens of seconds apart, past the ~20 s a RoCE wait allows before poisoning the runtime. Gathers captured in a graph never go through the barrier. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0065-glm-rank-checks` | v1.4 (#29); TF_GLM_MULTI_WATCHDOG_EXIT=0 by default | Seals the message rank 0 sends each iteration with a sequence number and a checksum (modulo the prime 2,147,483,647); rank 1 checks it and, if the ranks fall out of step, stops with an error naming the difference. /health gains iteration_s (how long the current step has run), and | Issue #29: a stalled server said nothing. It is not the fix (the cause was not found); it turns a desync into a named error and a stall into something a supervisor can restart. Same replies. In any SPMD system, silent disagreement between ranks becomes a hung collective; numbering and checking the control messages turns it into a readable | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `all-gather + soma em ordem de rank (comm.py)` | fp32 partials [R, 4096] per rank; NCCL through ctypes | Instead of an all-reduce, each rank produces fp32 partials after attention and after the MoE/MLP, runs ncclAllGather on the current stream (capturable in a graph) and sums rank 0 first; the head is split by vocabulary and the candidates are exchanged by all-gather. | A rank-order sum after the gather keeps the ranks bit-equal: an all-reduce does not guarantee that order, and both ranks must sample the same token without exchanging it. | 38 Parallelism, 04 Numbers in the computer |
| `comparação de configurações entre ranks` | a list of integers gathered by all-gather at startup | Each rank packs the relevant settings (drafter, capacity, TF_GLM_LATENT, PREFILL_ROWS, PROMPT_GRID, KVB, COPY_*, HC_SPLIT*, MTP, DRAFT_RING, MAX_ROWS, WIDE_GRAPHS, NUCLEUS_UNION…) and refuses to start if they differ: "the two ranks were started with different settings". | Fail-closed: the ranks run the same code and the same collectives, so any difference would become a hang or different bits; each patch that adds a knob adds it to the list. | 48 Exact engine (TensorFold), 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `cx7_peer (#9)` | automatic when FABRIC_PEER is empty | If the route to WORKER leaves through a port without RoCE, it looks for a worker CX7 address that a local CX7 port reaches directly (same subnet, no gateway) and uses it, logging so. | Fixes #9: prepare.sh picked the LAN port when WORKER was given by a LAN address. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `detect_link / link_info (GID RoCE v2)` | route → netdev → HCA (sysfs) → index of the RoCE v2 GID holding ::ffff: | Finds, on each node, the address, netdev, RDMA device and GID index from the route to the peer; the chosen GID is the RoCE v2 entry holding the node's IPv4. | An all-zero or v1 entry makes NCCL fail about a minute in. If an HCA or GID is missing, the script stops and asks for FABRIC_PEER. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `doorbell do rank 1 ocioso` | key tf_glm_request_ in the TCPStore | An idle rank 1 waits on the doorbell (a key in the rendezvous's own TCPStore), not inside an all-gather; rank 0 rings it for each request. | An idle server holds no GPU or CPU core. It was a Mia patch that went upstream in v0.6.0 (#132), so it left the patch list. | 48 Exact engine (TensorFold), 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `nccl_env` | NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME, NCCL_IB_HCA, NCCL_IB_GID_INDEX, NCCL_MIN/MAX_NCHANNELS=4 | Builds, per rank, NCCL's environment on the link: the bootstrap netdev, the RDMA rails, the GID index and 4 channels (+ NCCL_DEBUG, if set). | Measured: a decode all-gather in 32 µs (80 with the defaults) and a 32 MB chunk in 1.9 ms on two rails; NCCL's other knobs did not help. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `rails (dois trilhos)` | the link's HCA + the 2nd function on the same subnet, with a v2 GID at the same index | Lists the node's RoCE devices on the link's subnet (the second CX7 port too, if up and addressed) with a v2 GID at the same index; the list becomes NCCL_IB_HCA. | Each cabled QSFP port shows up as two PCIe Gen5 x4 functions; with two rails a prompt chunk's 32 MB all-gather takes 1.9 ms against 3.5 ms. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |

| `0038-glm-kv-fp8` | KV=fp8 → TF_GLM_KV=fp8 (patch: bf16) | The DSA latent and the indexer's pooled keys as FP8 rows [e4m3 codes \| fp32 scale \| 12 zero bytes], with scale 2^(ceil(log2 amax) − 8) (maximum in [128, 256] &lt; 448: nothing saturates); readers multiply the scale into the fp32 products (exact for a power of two). The indexer' | In bf16 the 1M window with 4 streams does not fit. 528 B/token/layer against vLLM's 656 for fp8_ds_mla; a 2.65% relative error per row, the same (±0.001) as fp32 per-token, per-block or per-layer scales. GSM8K 98.0%, HumanEval 97.6% (TR3 checkpoint, v1.3.2), 1M needle found; a pool of 2,922,496 tokens. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |

#### Kernels / CUDA

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0001-glm-exl3-prompt-experts` | TF_GLM_EXL3_NFIRST=1, TF_GLM_EXL3_PF=1 (windows ≥ 64 rows) | The grouped EXL3 kernel for routed experts in prompts: with NFIRST an item's column blocks (up to 16 pairs of rows routed to the same expert) are launched side by side; with PF, register double buffering of the next k step's trellis words and A fragments. | With the item on the grid's fastest dimension, re-reads of an item's rows were far apart in time and fell out of L2. Result: the same kernel and arithmetic, 2.3–2.5× faster at 4,096 rows; same bits. | 28 Kernels, 26 Inside the GPU |
| `0004-glm-prompt-kernels` | TF_GLM_EXL3_PROMPT, _PASS, _SEQ, TF_GLM_HC_MMA, TF_GLM_LATENT_MMA, TF_GLM_SEQ_ROWS, TF_GLM_PREFILL_ROWS (defaults) | EXL3 prompt kernels Yᵀ = Wᵀ Xᵀ: the decoded trellis tile becomes an A fragment in registers and feeds up to 64 tokens as B from shared memory, with a 3-stage cp.async pipeline, ldmatrix and fused epilogues (FWHT-128, SiLU with the reference's bf16 roundings). Also tensor cores in | Decoding the trellis again for each group of 16 rows wasted prefill work. One fp32 chain per row over ascending k (no split-K) gives bits independent of the chunking, other than the decode kernels'. SEQ in the BF16 matmuls: 1.3–3.2× on N ≥ 512, K ≤ 4,096 (2,048 rows, GB10). Vision workspace 1.5 → 0.75 GiB. | 28 Kernels, 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3 |
| `0016-glm-decode-kernels` | TF_GLM_Q4_TILE=table, TF_GLM_EXL3_DEC/_FUSE/_XROW=1, TF_GLM_KDA_WIDE_ROWS=1 | Decode kernels: a per-shape Q4 tile table measured on GB10 (split-K with clusters when possible); the EXL3 dec_kernel with a fused epilogue (FWHT-128 + svh_d in the down projection; an optional "last block" pattern for gate/up); KDA decode in three kernels (8 blocks per head) wit | Before, there was one tile per shape and epilogues with a round trip of fp32 Z through memory. KDA decode 4–36% faster at 1–8 rows; FUSE 2 and the unrolled loop measured slower. A graph keeps the addresses it was captured with, so outgrown buffers stay alive. Same bits. | 28 Kernels, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0019-glm-decode-kernels2` | TF_GLM_COMBINE_SY=1, TF_GLM_HC_DEC=1, TF_GLM_LATENT_STAGES=3, TF_GLM_Q4_TILE (configs 12–15) | More decode kernels: 32-column Q4 tiles, a copy-free MoE combine, hc_partial in CUDA with 48 blocks per row reproducing the Triton bits (layout, butterflies and sum order copied) and a 3-stage pipeline in latent attention. | No isolated number; it is part of the README's "faster decode matmuls, hyper-connection mixing" line. Same bits. | 28 Kernels, 15 Hyper-connections |
| `0020-glm-prompt-experts-order` | TF_GLM_EXL3_ORDER=1 | prompt_order_kernel (1,024 threads, a per-warp scan) evenly interleaves, in the prompt plan, "heavy" items (an expert with several items, weights via L2) and "light" ones (one item, read from DRAM); decode_tile_p decodes the trellis with fewer integer instructions (PRMT, funnel s | In plan order the two kinds came in waves, and DRAM and the tensor cores worked in turns. Only which block runs which item changes: every output keeps its bits. Part of 0010's ~1,270 → ~1,730 tok/s. | 28 Kernels, 11 Mixture-of-Experts |
| `0046-glm-l2-prefetch` | TF_GLM_L2PF=1 (bulk), _MB=8, _SITES=afo, _ROWS=64, _KB=32 | L2 prefetch on a side stream forked and joined inside the graph: at sites a/f/o, a small kernel asks the memory system for the weights the main stream reads next (cp.async.bulk.prefetch.L2, prefetch.global.L2::evict_last or ld.global.cg); the shared expert now runs before the rou | A decode layer alternates DRAM-bound matmuls with phases where DRAM sits idle (all-gathers, HC glue, router, KDA recurrence, latent core). One request: prose 48.36 → 49.46, code 59.54 → 61.08; adapted from jayleaton's patch 0460. Same bits. | 26 Inside the GPU, 28 Kernels |

Alone: prose 48.36 → 48.78, code 59.54 → 60.42; with 0046 + 512 KiB: +2.7% / +3.3% (4 requests: 74.8 → 76.6, 100.0 → 102.7). Adapted from jayleaton's patch 0580. Same bits. | 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3, 28 Kernels |

#### Launch / topology

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `image_hash / prebuilt_image` | sha256(cat patches/*.patch + IMAGE_EXTRAS), 12 hex | image_hash digests the patches and the pip extras; prebuilt_image returns $GHCR_IMAGE@$IMAGE_DIGEST when the v0.6.0-&lt;hash&gt; tag is the pinned one, else $GHCR_IMAGE:v0.6.0-&lt;hash&gt;. | Changing a patch changes the hash, the tag looked for and the kernel cache folder: the image is identified by its content, not by a hand-written version number. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `prepare.sh: build da imagem (Dockerfile inline)` | FROM nvcr.io/nvidia/pytorch:26.07-py3 + TensorFold@v0.6.0 + 53 patches (v1.3.2; 123 in the 4624c21 image) | With no usable image: pip install git+TF_REPO@TF_VERSION and the extras; COPY patches /opt/tf-patches; in the package directory, apply each *.patch in filename order with patch -p0 --forward (a failure fails the build); import the patched modules as a test; LABEL tf.patches=&lt;h | The patches are baked into the image, applied once at build time (the vLLM recipe reapplied overlays on every docker run); the TF install layer stays cached, so rebuilding after changing patches takes seconds. | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `prepare.sh: imagem no worker (image_ident)` | sha256 of RootFS.Layers + Env/Entrypoint/Cmd/WorkingDir | The worker pulls the same image or receives it through docker save \| ssh docker load (only missing layers); identity is compared by content (the layers' diffIDs + runtime config). | .Id is the manifest digest under containerd and the config digest under overlay2, so it never matched across mixed stores (#8). | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `scripts/config.sh (precedência)` | environment > scripts/local.sh > ./.env > defaults | The first that sets a value wins: the environment; scripts/local.sh (sourced as bash, but the environment wins over what it changed); ./.env (KEY=value lines, quotes and comments handled, read and never run; where both set a key, local.sh wins); then the defaults. | local.sh and .env belong to the user, outside git, so updating the repository does not overwrite each setup's configuration. | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `scripts/publish-image.sh` | :v0.6.0- and :latest on GHCR | Checks that the tf.patches label matches image_hash, re-tags with OCI labels (org.opencontainers.image.source = the repository), logs in with a throwaway DOCKER_CONFIG, pushes and prints the IMAGE_TAG/IMAGE_DIGEST to pin. | The package inherits the repository's visibility and no credential stays in ~/.docker; it needs a token with write:packages (GHCR_TOKEN, GITHUB_TOKEN or gh auth). | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `start-tp3.sh` | TP=3, COMM=nccl; PARALLEL 8, KV_POOL_GIB 32; patches 0066–0068 (v1.4, experimental) | Runs the same recipe as tensor parallel over 3 Sparks: exports TP=3 and COMM=nccl and calls start.sh. The --tp N engine comes from patches 0066–0068 (TensorFold v0.6.0 serves two ranks only): heads, expert columns, vocabulary and DFlash2 KV groups split in whole units, the remain | Against 2 Sparks (sparkDash, 4 streams): one request's prose 60.4 → 77.6 tok/s, four at once 108.8 → 146.2; prefill about the same (1–4% faster). With the 8-request default (v1.5): 166.0 tok/s prose and 211.5 code in all. Replies equal to 2 Sparks' and to serial ones. In v1.3.2 the engine served 2 ranks only; the 4th Spark lives on a tp4  | 40 TP=3 & TP=4, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `start.sh: checagem de argumentos` | docker run --rm … tensorfold.cli.build_parser().parse_args(...) | Before stopping anything, it validates tensorfold serve with all arguments in a throwaway container without the GPU, using TensorFold's own parser; start.sh also validates its own variables (DRAFTER, DENSE, KV, PARALLEL 1–4, COPY_MAX 1–15, DRAFT_POLICY f*…). | A typo fails before restart takes down the running server: nothing is changed. Extra arguments go to both ranks after the defaults and win (argparse keeps the last value). | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `start.sh: docker run dos ranks` | --gpus all --ipc=host --network host --shm-size 16g --device /dev/infiniband --cap-add IPC_LOCK --ulimit memlock=-1 --ulimit stack=67108864 | First rank 1 on the worker over ssh, then rank 0 + the API on the head: tensorfold serve &lt;snapshot&gt; --tp 2 --rank R --master &lt;HEAD_ADDR&gt; --master-port 29551 …, with the HF cache and the kernel cache mounted. | (interpretation) --network host for the CX7 link and the rendezvous; /dev/infiniband + IPC_LOCK + unlimited memlock to register pinned memory for RDMA; --ipc=host and a 16 GB shm for shared memory; a large stack for threads. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `start.sh: progresso da carga (gpu_gib)` | every 15 s | Shows the elapsed time and how much of the startup estimate (startup estimate in the log) is already on each Spark's GPU (nvidia-smi --query-compute-apps filtered by the container's PIDs). | Loading ~80 GiB a Spark takes 2–6 min; if a rank exits, start.sh shows both ranks' last log lines and why. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `start.sh: repasse de TENSORFOLD_*/TF_GLM_*/TF_ROCE_*` | -e on both containers + HF_HUB_OFFLINE | Every TENSORFOLD_*, TF_GLM_* and TF_ROCE_* variable present in the environment becomes -e NAME=value on both docker run commands. | Rank 1's command runs over ssh, where the local environment does not reach, and both ranks compare their settings at startup and refuse to start if they differ. None of them is a secret. | 48 Exact engine (TensorFold), 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `start.sh: smoke test` | max_tokens 32, temperature 0, enable_thinking false, "Reply with OK." | One chat completion through both ranks; a reply without text fails the start; then it prints GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3 is now LIVE! on port 8888 and a summary of the configuration. | Thinking off and greedy so a short reply has text (otherwise the model thinks first). | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 25 Measuring quality |
| `stop.sh` | STOP_TIMEOUT=30 | Stops and removes the containers on both Sparks in parallel, freeing their GPU memory, and warns how many running requests (/health, requests_running) will be cut off. | It does not drain; without access to the worker, it leaves rank 1 alone and warns. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `v0.6.0-789e8f3bdfa2 · 123 patches` | IMAGE_TAG v0.6.0-789e8f3bdfa2, IMAGE_DIGEST sha256:99fe7496…69956 (4624c21, 2026-10-25) | The published image the recipe pins today: TensorFold v0.6.0 + 123 patches (numbered 0001 to 0129, with gaps), pulled by digest; the same image serves 2 and 3 Sparks. v1.3.2 pinned v0.6.0-ae8d1c789b47, with 53 patches. | The 70 extra patches bring what v1.3.2 lacked: 3 Sparks (0066–0068), up to 8 requests (0069), sliced fill and smooth streaming (0061–0062), the Anthropic API (0084), a spill tier (0088, 0128), kept-state limits (0089, 0097, 0119), explicit health (0118), KV sharding (0129). The count that holds is the image's: the README intro still says  | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 41 Recipe engineering |

No isolated number ("less memory at 1M"). | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 28 Kernels |

Read once, at start, and logged. | On kernel 6.7+ a bulk free (prepare.sh's docker save / docker load) parks 5–9 GiB on those lists, which come back at ~0.05–0.1 GiB/s over 1–2 min; the first start of a new image landed in that window: rank 0 budgeted 89.44 GiB against 94.86 on a warm restart and kept 2 prompt states instead of 32. With 0122, 94.58 GiB (rank 1 95.20). It o | 27 Unified memory, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `orçamento de memória (capacity.py)` | MemAvailable − reserve (integrated GPU) | available_bytes: on GB10 (integrated) the available memory is MemAvailable from /proc/meminfo minus the reserve; on a discrete GPU, the lesser of the GPU's and the host's free memory. A setting that does not fit is refused before loading weights, with the largest window that fits | The server's allocations come out of host RAM but are not charged to the container's memory limit (docker run --memory), and exhausting unified memory can freeze the host. Rank 0's startup estimate: 88.09 GiB. | 27 Unified memory, 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |
| `start.sh: refit da janela` | one retry with --context N | If rank 0 exits before it is ready and the log says largest fitting prompt-plus-reply window: N tokens, start.sh stops everything and restarts once with --context N, with a warning. | On the Spark's unified memory, running out tends to freeze the machine rather than fail an allocation: TensorFold refuses before loading the weights, and the launcher uses the answer. | 27 Unified memory, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |

| `0008-glm-prompt-grid` | TF_GLM_PROMPT_GRID (0 in the patch; 64 with KDA_CHUNKED=1) | Prompt states kept only at multiples of G: the request prefills up to B = ⌊n/G⌋·G with no head and no first token, takes the snapshot and resumes from it up to n; the Cut machinery that cut the state inside a chunk is removed. | With kernels whose bits depend on block alignment (the chunked KDA), resuming from an arbitrary point would give bits different from a fresh prefill. No isolated number; it is part of prompt reuse (identical 64k 34 s → &lt; 0.07 s; 7.9k system 4.24 → 0.13 s). | 33 Prefix caching, 14 Linear attention & hybrids |
| `0015-glm-shared-prefix` | TF_GLM_SHARED_PREFIX (0 → 1 = 256 tokens via SHARED_PREFIX) | shared_points: besides the end point, up to 2 extra states per request, at the longest prefix shared with a kept prompt and at the first &lt;\|user\|&gt; (end of the system block), each on the grid and only if it adds ≥ 256 tokens; rank 0 picks the points and sends them in the he | v0.6.0 kept only the prompt's end, so a new conversation with the same system prompt reused nothing. A 7.9k system prompt: 4.24 → 0.13 s, same replies. | 33 Prefix caching, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `0042-glm-prompt-replay` | no knob of its own (kept states; TF_GLM_CACHE_ENTRIES) | The snapshot of a prompt's end state also keeps the head's logits row (this rank's slice, before any grammar mask); an identical prompt resumes there and samples the first token from that row, with no prefill. | A state alone does not give the first token, so an identical resend still redid the last chunk. Identical 64k: 34 s → &lt; 0.07 s, with the same reply (the same key). Prompts with images and requests with a grammar do not replay. | 33 Prefix caching |

#### Quantization

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0002-glm-dense-fp8` | TF_GLM_DENSE=fp8 (recipe: DENSE=q4; fp8 optional) | The checkpoint's BF16 projections (attention, shared experts, dense MLPs, head) → FP8 e4m3 at load, with an fp32 scale = absmax/448 per row × 128-column block; a Triton _fmm kernel with the BF16 _bmm's structure (independent rows, split-K fixed by shape) and the scale applied at  | That is ~9.7 GiB a Spark read at every bandwidth-bound decode step: half bf16's bytes, ~+33% decode. The head verifies in FP8, and drafts use a separate 4-bit copy. Lossy against bf16. | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 20 Quantization fundamentals |
| `0005-glm-dense-q4` | TF_GLM_DENSE=q4 (recipe: DENSE=q4, default) | quantize4_mse: per group of 64 values it tries 16 clippings a = 1 − 0.45·i/15 (1.0 to 0.55 of min/max), with scale (hi − lo)/15 and bias lo in bf16, and keeps the one with the least squared error; it packs 8 nibbles per int32 in the Q4 kernels' MLX layout. The head stays in FP8 ( | Over fp8: prose 38.9 → 44.4 tok/s, code 44.2 → 48.7, prefill ~1,090 → ~1,260 tok/s; GSM8K 98.0% and HumanEval 95.1%, the same as fp8 (TR3 checkpoint). Being affine, x·(s·q + b) = s·(x·q) + b·Σx, which is why the kernels take per-group sums. Regression #18: end of turn lost on short French prompts (12/48). | 20 Quantization fundamentals, 25 Measuring quality |

#### Reasoning / tools

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0023-server-effort-max` | reasoning_effort: "max" (medium → high, xhigh → max) | EFFORTS gains max, mapped to the template's top level when the template names it (GLM-5.3 names max, its default), else to xhigh; medium becomes high when the template names high but not medium. | Clients ask for efforts under different names; with no effort GLM thinks at Max, and "none" answers without thinking. | 09 Generation & sampling, 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `0036-glm-tool-calls` | TF_GLM_KEEP_REASONING=1024, TF_GLM_KEEP_REASONING_MB=32 | GLM tool calls for agents: GlmCallStreamer turns &lt;tool_call&gt;…&lt;arg_key&gt;…&lt;arg_value&gt; into OpenAI deltas; ThinkSplit counts calls written inside the think block only if they end the reply; KeptReasoning puts back the reasoning the client dropped; agent histories sa | Long calls sent nothing for minutes (idle cut-offs in LiteLLM and Node's fetch), and a dropped reasoning breaks the prompt prefix, making the cache re-read the rest. Parts adapted from jayleaton's patch 0620. A feature (checked by tools/toolcheck.py). | 34 API, tools & JSON, 33 Prefix caching |
| `0051-glm-tool-history-recovery` | no setting | A past tool call whose arguments are not a JSON object is left out of the prompt together with its result (matched by id or, without ids, by position) and logged; the message keeps its other calls, content and reasoning. | Fixes #2: agents resend the history, so an HTTP 400 over corrupted arguments blocked the conversation for good. | 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `0053-glm-whole-tool-calls` | constant CALL_KEEPALIVE_S = 2.0 (s) | Each tool call is sent whole (header + arguments in one delta) when &lt;/tool_call&gt; arrives; a call the reply ends inside is never sent (the reply ends with length); while a call is held, an empty delta goes out every 2 s. | With 0036's argument deltas, a reply cut by max_tokens inside a call sent cut arguments that a client might store or run; the keepalive serves clients with an idle timeout. | 34 API, tools & JSON |

#### Sampling / generation

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0034-cuda-nucleus-union` | TENSORFOLD_NUCLEUS_UNION=1 (off by default) | The top-p coverage test over all ranks' candidates together: it walks them merged up to mass need and stops at v*; if v* is above the last candidate of each rank that did not send its whole shard, the walk is the whole vocabulary's; otherwise it tries 16 × NUCLEUS before reading  | The per-rank test requires each rank's share of the nucleus to fit in its own candidates (conservative) and often reads whole shards. The same nucleus, the same draw; opt-in, no number. | 09 Generation & sampling, 38 Parallelism |
| `amostragem com chave (exact_sampling)` | the request's seed or the SHA-256 of the prompt ids; float64 on the host | An indexable uniform u = f(seed, absolute position, token id) via splitmix64; sorted candidates, top-k → top-p → min-p cuts, Gumbel noise −log(−log u) and argmax. Verifying a draft means sampling that position with the key and comparing. | The drafted reply is bit for bit the serial one with any drafter or policy, and without a seed the same request gives the same reply. By contrast, vLLM's rejection sampling preserves the distribution, not the sequence. | 09 Generation & sampling, 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |

Prose ×4 103.4 → 108.8 tok/s, TTFT 590 → 340 ms; structured ×3 175.2 → 196.3, ×4 196.3 → 227.9; exact. | 32 Scheduler & batching, 10 Prefill, decode & KV |

#### Speculative decoding

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0007-glm-copy-drafts` | TF_GLM_COPY_DRAFTS (0 → 1 via COPY), TF_GLM_COPY_MATCH=8, TF_GLM_COPY_MAX (5 → 15) | Copy drafts (prompt lookup): CopyDrafts finds, vectorized in numpy, the earlier occurrences of the last 8 tokens and proposes what followed the most recent one; in the mtp/dflash/auto loops the proposal replaces the drafter's chain for that round. | Quotes, edits and repetitions have predictable continuations without a model; both ranks compute the same proposals with no exchange. Quote / edit 80.3 → 84.4 tok/s, prose and code unchanged; exact. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `0011-glm-draft-sim` | TF_GLM_DRAFT_DUMP (off), TF_GLM_DRAFT_QUANT=minmax | A teacher-forced DFlash2 dump (it decodes one token a round and records candidates, logits, selector edges, the keyed Gumbel noise and the committed token) + an offline draft_sim simulator (numpy) of fc, nc, cost and oracle policies, with a VERIFY_MS cost table (29.2…66.0 ms for  | Tuning stop rules live is costly and noisy. With keyed sampling, a round starting at position i keeps exactly the drafts equal to the committed tokens, so any policy is replayed exactly: that is how the stop rules were tuned. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `0013-glm-decode-rounds` | MAX_ROWS 8 → 16; COPY_MAX up to 15; TF_GLM_KVB=bf16 | Decode rounds: verify windows of up to 16 rows (graphs for 1–8, wider ones eager); _Clock times the window with CUDA events and no extra sync; _Late hands tokens to the server one round later, with the next window already on the GPU; DFlash2's candidates in a single non-blocking  | Each round had a synchronize(), two pageable .cpu() copies and token processing with the GPU idle. With COPY_MAX 15: edit 84 → 119 tok/s, prose and code unchanged; exact. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `0018-glm-noise-policies` | fncN:P[:B] and fcostN:noisy[:B] policies; recipe fnc7:0.3 | Noise-aware DFlash2 stop rules: the chain picks argmax(score + NOISE·g) with EDGE = 0.6, NOISE = 0.7 and g the target's keyed Gumbel noise; confidence becomes softmax((score + g)/β), with β = 2.0; fnc stops when the product drops below P; fcost cuts at the k that maximizes E[toke | The target samples with noise that is known before verification. β fitted by log loss on the 9 teacher-forced dumps of 2026-09-30 (2.0 on all). fnc7:0.3 over fc5:0.3: prose 47.2 → 50.6, code 51.7 → 55.5 tok/s. Only where the chain stops changes: the picks are the same. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 09 Generation & sampling |
| `0021-glm-dflash-policy-env` | TF_GLM_DFLASH_POLICY (fc5:0.3 → fnc7:0.3 via DRAFT_POLICY) | The default DFlash2 policy by environment variable, validated at boot as a DFlash2 policy. | Lets the recipe pick the stop rule without touching code; the measured effect is 0018's. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `0025-glm-dflash2-ring` | TF_GLM_DRAFT_RING=1 (from v0.6.0) | With DFlash2's context in a 2,176-row ring, a snapshot cannot copy kc/vc[:, lo:n] by slice: _saved_views no longer includes the drafter's views, because ring snapshots already carry drafter_rows. | Fixes kept prompt states with shared prefixes under the ring: DFlash2's window goes along with the state without copying the whole window. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 33 Prefix caching |
| `0027-glm-multi-dflash2` | PARALLEL > 1 with DRAFTER=dflash2; TF_GLM_MULTI_DRAFT_GRAPHS=1 | DFlash2 for several streams: MultiDrafter shares the weights and creates a context pool [kv_heads, streams·cap + trash, head_dim]; a batched block pass with a segmented dynamic conv (SEG) and attention segmented by a slot/position table; the selector's projector (cuBLAS) and torc | One pass per stream would multiply launches per round. Operations that may pick a kernel by row count run on each stream's own rows, so each stream's candidates are its solo drafter's bits. No isolated number. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `0032-glm-code-copy-drafts` | COPY_CODE=1 → TF_GLM_WIDE_GRAPHS=16, TF_GLM_COPY_REPLY_MATCH=16; TF_GLM_COPY_MISS_MAX=0; TF_GLM_MAX_ROWS=16 | Copy drafts for code: copies from the reply itself must match N tokens; an optional back-off after a wrong copy round; windows between 8 rows and a captured width are padded by repeating the last draft so they run as a CUDA graph; a configurable widest window from 16 to 64. | Over 39 real code replies (2026-09-30), copies from the reply kept 23% of drafts and copies from the prompt 74%; windows over 8 rows ran without a graph. Code 55.4 → 56.2 tok/s; edit 120 → 125. Exact. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |

| `STREAM_SMOOTH / STREAM_SMOOTH_MS` | 1 / 400 (ms); 0 = one event a round, as in v1.3.2 | Smooth streaming (0061): with drafts a round accepts ~3 tokens at once, so a reply arrives in bursts (every ~50 ms alone, ~100 ms with 4 streams). With 1, tokens go out one event each at a steady pace from a STREAM_SMOOTH_MS playout buffer; text appears that much later, the reply | Without a concurrent fill (2 Sparks): one reply, gaps p50 / p90 50 / 68 → 16 / 20 ms; four at once 107 / 128 → 40 / 58 ms; the same text. Together with FILL_BUDGET_MS it protects the replies already streaming. It applies to any server with speculative decoding: the cadence a user sees is the verification round's, not the token's (interpre | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 32 Scheduler & batching |

#### Attention / kernels

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KDA_CHUNKED` | 1 | KDA prompt chunks in chunked (WY) form, one CUDA kernel of 32-row sub-chunks (patches 0012, 0014, 0039; → TF_GLM_KDA_CHUNKED); prompt states then sit on a 64-token grid. | 50k prefill 29.3 → 26.4 s, 149k 91.1 → 84.5 s (one boot each). Close to the serial kernel, not its bits: prompt arithmetic changes, but drafted = serial still holds. DENSE=bf16 KV=bf16 KDA_CHUNKED=0 serves the checkpoint as it is. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `TF_GLM_KDA_BV` | 16 in 0012; removed by 0039 (not read in the final image) | Value rows of the 128×128 state per sequential program in the Triton chunked KDA (0012/0014). | The geometry of the state pass, the only sequential part of the chunked form. In 0039's CUDA kernel the geometry is fixed (one 256-thread block per head, 32-row sub-chunks). | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_KDA_CHUNKED` | 0 in the patch; 1 in the recipe (KDA_CHUNKED) | The engine switch: with 1, the 34 KDA layers' prefill uses the chunked kernel and an empty or 0 TF_GLM_PROMPT_GRID becomes 64 (32, 64 and 128 accepted). Decode stays on the serial kernel. | A row's bits depend only on its 32-row sub-chunk and its input state; hence chunks must start on multiples of 32 so that a resumed prompt = a fresh prompt. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_KDA_KSPLIT` | 4 in 0012; removed by 0039 (not read in the final image) | K slices of the 16 × 16 × 128 products in the Triton chunked KDA (1: a single dot). | A geometry knob of the Triton version, deleted together with it by 0039. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_KDA_PREC` | tf32x3 (tf32, ieee) in 0012; removed by 0039 (not read in the final image) | The tl.dot input precision in the Triton kernels of the first chunked KDA version (0012/0014). | tf32x3 ≈ fp32 (~2e-6 relative to float64); tf32 ~5e-4, half a bf16 step. 0039 replaced the Triton kernels with a CUDA kernel that sums in fp32 with explicit FMAs and deleted this read; the research's internal-knob table still lists it. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 04 Numbers in the computer |
| `TF_GLM_KDA_STATE_ROWS` | 32 in 0012; removed by 0039 (not read in the final image) | Rows per step of the sequential state kernel (_kda_state) in the Triton chunked KDA (0012/0014). | A geometry knob of the Triton version; 0039's CUDA kernel walks 32-row sub-chunks by construction. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_SCORE_RB` | 4 (1, 2, 4 or 8) | Rows per program in the prompt's pool-scoring kernel (_scores), which loads each pool tile once for the RB rows. | Speed only: the scores and the top-512 do not change. | 13 Sparse attention |
| `TF_GLM_SPARSE_ONEPASS` | 1 | One-pass sparse latent attention for prompts: one program per (row, group of 32 heads, latent slice) walks all selected tokens in a single online softmax and writes bf16, with no partials or merge. | Fixed (32, 32, 1) tile: a row's bits do not depend on the chunk (they differ from sparse_attention's). With 0028, the memory estimate stops counting the partials this kernel does not allocate. | 13 Sparse attention, 28 Kernels |

#### Distributed / network

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `COMM` | roce (or nccl) | Transport of the ranks' all-gathers (→ TF_GLM_COMM): roce sends the small ones (a decode round's partials, the samplers; up to TF_ROCE_MAX_KB) as one-shot RDMA writes over the RoCE link (b12x's transport, patch 0006), and NCCL keeps the rest; nccl: NCCL for all. | 11 µs per 16 KiB gather against NCCL's 45; decode +6% (prose 44.2 → 46.9, code 48.7 → 51.6). Same bits. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `FABRIC_PEER` | empty (uses WORKER's address) | The worker's CX7 address when WORKER is reached over another network. The ranks (NCCL, rendezvous and RoCE) talk over the route to that address. | Since v1.3.1, when the route to WORKER leaves through a port without RoCE and FABRIC_PEER is empty, cx7_peer finds a directly reachable CX7 address of the worker by itself (#9); setting it by hand skips the detection. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `MASTER_PORT` | 29551 | The ranks' rendezvous port (--master-port): rank 0 opens a torch.distributed.TCPStore there and publishes the ncclUniqueId; rank 1 reads it and both call ncclCommInitRank. | Neither the rendezvous port nor the HTTP server is authenticated: keep it on the private link. The same store is the idle rank 1's doorbell. | 48 Exact engine (TensorFold), 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_CHANNELS` | 4 (→ NCCL_MIN_NCHANNELS = NCCL_MAX_NCHANNELS) | Each rank's NCCL channels, passed as equal minimum and maximum. | Measured between the Sparks: a decode-sized all-gather in 32 µs (80 with NCCL's defaults). Other knobs (protocols, buffers, QPs, NCCL_NET=IB) were no better, and some kept NCCL on one rail. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_RAILS` | 2 implied (1 = one CX7 port) | 1 forces a single rail (the link's HCA) even with both CX7 ports up and addressed on the link's subnet. | With two rails a prompt chunk's all-gather (32 MB) takes 1.9 ms against 3.5 ms on one (~1.8×). If the two ends have different rail counts, the script falls back to one. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `SPLIT` | 1 (→ TF_GLM_HC_SPLIT=1, TF_GLM_PREFILL_OVERLAP=2; 0 → 0/0) | Splits the prompt chunks' hyper-connection glue by rows between the two Sparks, with the exchanges overlapped with the next rows' work and the next block's "front" (patches 0010, 0033). | 50k prefill ~1,270 → ~1,730 tok/s (with 0009 and 0020); a 149k prompt 92.8 → 90.7 s with overlap 2; decode rounds pay ~1.5%. Same bits. | 15 Hyper-connections, 38 Parallelism |
| `TF_GLM_COMM` | nccl in the patch; roce in the recipe (COMM) | The engine switch: with roce the engine wraps NCCL in a RoceComm; contiguous gathers of up to max_bytes go over RoCE, the rest over NCCL with the same output layout (rank order). | Since patch 0040 eligibility depends only on the byte count, equal on both ranks; the handshake (QPs, rkeys, GIDs) goes through NCCL itself. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TF_GLM_HC_EXCHANGE` | p2p (or gather) | p2p: swaps the split's rows with grouped ncclSend/ncclRecv; gather: uses NCCL's all-gather with the same volume. | gather is for fabrics where p2p is poor; it uses plain NCCL because the RoCE kernel is not written for in-place use. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 38 Parallelism |
| `TF_GLM_HC_SPLIT` | 0 in the patch; 1 in the recipe (SPLIT) | Rank r owns rows [r·H, r·H + H) (H = R/2; an odd chunk gets a zero row): it sends the peer the fp32 partial of the peer's rows (ncclSend/ncclRecv), sums the two partials of its own rows rank 0 first, runs hc_post + the next hc_pre only on them and swaps the normalized rows (bf16) | Every glue kernel is row-independent and the partial-sum order is unchanged, so every bit is the unsplit path's. Prefill only; decode keeps the replicated glue. | 15 Hyper-connections, 38 Parallelism |
| `TF_GLM_HC_SPLIT_MIN_ROWS` | 64 (2–16384) | Prompt chunks with fewer rows (a prompt's tail) run unsplit. | With few rows the exchange does not pay for the glue it saves (interpretation). | 15 Hyper-connections, 38 Parallelism |
| `TF_GLM_OVERLAP_PIECES` | 2 (1–8) | Row pieces each split exchange runs in on the side stream (overlap, p2p only): each exchange starts when its partial's piece is done, and each piece's glue waits only for its own partial. | More pieces overlap more, but each piece is one more exchange (interpretation); the recipe uses the default. | 15 Hyper-connections, 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TF_GLM_OVERLAP_PRIORITY` | 0 = normal since 0022 (−1 = high; it was −1 in 0017) | The priority of the side stream that runs the split's overlapped exchanges. | A high-priority stream in the process can cost decode rounds a little (graphs on the default stream): on two Sparks, two boots each, prefill was the same and decode after a high-priority prompt ~0.5–1% slower. Hence the normal default. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup, 45 Benchmarking |
| `TF_GLM_PREFILL_OVERLAP` | 0 in the patch; 2 in the recipe with SPLIT=1 (0 = none, 1 = overlapped exchanges, 2 = + fronts) | 1: the split's exchanges run on a second CUDA stream in row pieces, with events ordering the streams. 2 (patch 0033): during the exchanges the main stream runs the next block's front on its own rows (KDA projections, DSA q/kv and q_b, MLP gate/up or the shared expert). | Needs TF_GLM_HC_SPLIT=1. Level 2: a 149k prompt 92.8 → 90.7 s (with COPY_CODE, two boots each); same bits because the prompt matmul gives the same bits for any row slicing. | 15 Hyper-connections, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `TF_NCCL_LIB` | not set (found automatically) | Path to libnccl.so.2 when it is not found: TensorFold loads NCCL through ctypes, without torch.distributed process groups. | start.sh does not pass it on (the filter only forwards TENSORFOLD_*, TF_GLM_* and TF_ROCE_* to the containers); in NVIDIA's PyTorch container NCCL is already found (interpretation). | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |
| `TF_ROCE_GID_INDEX` | NCCL_IB_GID_INDEX, else 3 | The RoCE v2 GID index the one-shot transport's QPs use. | nodes.sh finds the index of the RoCE v2 entry holding the node's IPv4 and passes it as NCCL_IB_GID_INDEX; an all-zero or v1 entry makes NCCL fail about a minute in. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TF_ROCE_HCA` | NCCL_IB_HCA, else the active ports in /sys/class/infiniband (up to 2) | The HCAs the RoCE transport uses; the proxy splits each payload into stripes across up to 2 HCAs (the CX7 port's two PCIe functions). | When unset, it inherits the rails nodes.sh passed to NCCL, so RoCE and NCCL use the same devices. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TF_ROCE_MAX_KB` | 512 (TF/patch: 256) | The largest all-gather (KiB) that goes over RoCE. One row of partials = 4,096 fp32 = 16 KiB, so 512 KiB = 32 rows = the whole batched window of 4 streams. | With 512 the 17–32-row windows of concurrent requests also go over RoCE: code at 4 streams +1.4%, prose +0.5–1% (two boots each). Same bits. Just a setting, no patch of its own. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `TF_ROCE_WAIT_S` | v1.3.2: 20 (1–3600) · now 300 in the recipe (v1.6, #54); the patch still defaults to 20 | Seconds a GPU wait for the peer's shard may last before it poisons the runtime (default: SPIN_LIMIT = 20,000,000 polls of ~1 µs). | On a first start each rank builds its extensions on its own and they can drift tens of seconds apart (#6). Until the engine is built, each eager RoCE gather first goes through an NCCL barrier; graphs keep the value they were captured with. Issue #54: on long prompts (~475k–500k tokens, or after many hours) a rank failed after its 20 s wit | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |

#### KV cache

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KV` | fp8 (or bf16) | Format of the DSA latent cache and the indexer's pooled keys (→ TF_GLM_KV): fp8 = e4m3 rows with a power-of-two scale (528 B/token/layer); bf16 = exact. | With fp8 the 1M window with 4 streams fits (rank 0: 88.09 GiB estimated; pool of 2,922,496 tokens). Lossy: GSM8K 98.0%, HumanEval 97.6% (TR3 checkpoint, v1.3.2), 1M needle found; drafted = serial still holds. With bf16: ~196k tokens with DFlash2. With the current checkpoint and the same defaults the README reports GSM8K 98.8% (250 problem | 23 Quantized KV cache, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
| `TF_GLM_KV` | bf16 in the patch; fp8 in the recipe (KV) | The engine switch read by kv8.kv_kind: bf16 or fp8. In fp8 each row becomes uint8 [width + 16]: e4m3 codes + an fp32 scale + 12 zero bytes, with SHIFT = 8. | Needs the latent cache (TF_GLM_LATENT=1) and joins the ranks' settings comparison; one helper (store_row) quantizes prefill and decode, so drafted = serial and resumed = fresh. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 12 MLA & compact KV |
| `TF_GLM_KV_SHARD` | 0 (off); 1 turns it on (patch 0129; PARALLEL > 1, not with TF_GLM_MULTI_LONE) | Stores each conversation's MLA latent cache once, on its owner Spark, instead of on every Spark: tensor parallelism splits the heads, but every head reads the whole latent, so each Spark held a copy. In decode the queries are all-gathered and the owner computes all 64 heads over  | 11 DSA layers × (528 B of fp8 latent + 36 of keys) = 6,204 B per token per Spark → 3,300 B with 2 Sparks. 2 Sparks, PARALLEL=8, replies byte-equal to the unsharded engine: pool 1.51M → 2.83M tokens (1.87×; 3.47M with DISPLAY_KV_MIB=1792), decode at 8 streams 103.1 → 102.4 tok/s, a 205k prompt 113.4 → 113.7 s (borrowing rows) or 116.0 s (s | 38 Parallelism, 12 MLA & compact KV |
| `TF_GLM_LATENT` | 1 (0 = per-head K/V) | The latent cache: keeps only the normalized 512-wide latent c per token and DSA layer (1 KB a token and layer in bf16), absorbs the query once per row and expands the attended latent once. | The DSA layers are NoPE MLA, so score_h = (Wk_hᵀ q_h)·c and there is no RoPE to keep. FP8 KV (0038) and multi-prefill (0049) need the latent cache; with 0 the kv_k/kv_v copies come back. | 12 MLA & compact KV, 23 Quantized KV cache |

#### Kernels / CUDA

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_DEC` | 1 | The EXL3 decode dec_kernel: the grouped kernel's arithmetic (with prefetch) and a fused epilogue. | In the down projection (FUSE 1) the block's 128 columns are one Hadamard block: it applies fwht128 + svh_d to its own sums and writes Y, with no Z round trip and no epilogue launch. | 28 Kernels, 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3 |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_FUSE` | 1 (0–3) | Which epilogues to fuse in the dec_kernel: 1 = down only; 2 = gate/up, with the last of the 2·SK blocks to finish (an atomic counter after __threadfence) running the epilogue. | FUSE 2 measured slower, so the default fuses only the down projection. | 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_LOADS` | nc in the recipe (0/ldg, 1/nc/nc1, nc2, nc4; patch: 0) | The EXL3 trellis loads in the expert decode kernel (patch 0047, adapted from jayleaton's patch 0580): nc = 16-byte ld.global.nc.L1::no_allocate loads one k step ahead (nc2/nc4: 2 or 4 steps); 0 = TensorFold's 32-bit loads. | Alone: prose 48.36 → 48.78 tok/s, code 59.54 → 60.42. With TF_GLM_L2PF=1 and TF_ROCE_MAX_KB=512: one request prose 49.68, code 61.49 (+2.7% / +3.3%); 4 at once prose 74.8 → 76.6, code 100.0 → 102.7 tok/s (two boots each). Same bits. | 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_NFIRST` | 1 | The "n first" launch order in the grouped EXL3 kernel for windows of 64 rows or more: item = blockIdx.y, nblock = blockIdx.x, so an item's column blocks run side by side. | They read the item's rows while L2 still holds them: the same kernel and arithmetic, 2.3–2.5× faster at 4,096 rows. After 0004 it applies to 16-pair plans (the default prompt path uses the Yᵀ = WᵀXᵀ kernels). | 28 Kernels, 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_ORDER` | 1 | prompt_order_kernel evenly interleaves "heavy" items (an expert with several items, weights via L2, tensor-core bound) and "light" ones (one item, read from DRAM). | Keeps DRAM and the tensor cores busy together instead of in turns: only which block runs which item changes, every output keeps its bits. Part of SPLIT's gain (~1,270 → ~1,730 tok/s at 50k, with 0009). | 11 Mixture-of-Experts, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_PASS` | 64 (or 128) | Members per pass of the EXL3 prompt kernel: 64 (8 warps, 2 blocks per SM) or 128 (16 warps, 1 block per SM). | Same bits; 128 measured slower on real routing: most passes are small, and one 16-warp block per SM keeps fewer experts in flight. | 28 Kernels, 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_PF` | 1 | Register prefetch (double buffering) in the EXL3 kernel: the trellis words and A fragments of step kt+1 are loaded before step kt's decode and mma16816. | The order of the mma calls does not change, so neither do the bits; reused in the decode dec_kernel (0016). | 28 Kernels, 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3 |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_PROMPT` | 1 | Prompt chunks use the Yᵀ = Wᵀ Xᵀ kernels on a 64-pair plan: each warp decodes its trellis tiles straight into registers as A fragments, and each fragment feeds up to 64 tokens as B (8 mma per tile instead of 2). | One fp32 chain per row over ascending k, no split-K: bits independent of the chunking (but not the decode kernels'). With 0, chunks go back to the decode kernels. | 28 Kernels, 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3 |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_ROWROT` | 1 | The EXL3 input rotation (x·suh @ H) once per row in prompts, when shared_suh confirms bit for bit that every expert has the same gate and up suh. | With top-8 and 2 matrices, 16 rotations per row become 1 [estimate from the code]; same bits. | 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_SEQ` | 1 | In the grouped kernel, one program walks the SK splits in sequence and keeps the totals in registers (((0 + s0) + s1) + …). | The same bits without the SK fp32 partials' round trip through memory: it is the epilogue's own sum, without the partials' traffic. | 28 Kernels, 04 Numbers in the computer |
| `TF_GLM_EXL3_XROW` | 1 | With the shared suh (0009), the input rotation is also done once per row in decode. | Cuts redundant work without changing bits. | 28 Kernels, 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3 |
| `TF_GLM_HC_DEC` | 1 | hc_partial (the first half of hc_pre) in CUDA for decode rows: 48 blocks per row instead of 16, 288 threads, weights read once for up to 4 rows. | It reproduces the bits of the Triton _hc_partial kernel by copying its layout and reduction tree (butterflies, __fmaf_rn/__fadd_rn). | 15 Hyper-connections, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_HC_MMA` | 1 | The hc_pre's 24 mixing products and the sum of squares of prompt rows on the tensor cores (tl.dot, BM = BK = 64). | Faster prefill; it changes the prompt's bits (not the decode's), keeping drafted = serial and chunking independence. | 15 Hyper-connections, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_KDA_WIDE_ROWS` | 1 (64 = earlier behavior) | Windows of at least this many rows run the KDA chain in three kernels (prep/step/out: 8 blocks per head instead of one, with chain_kernel's bits). | With 1, every decode window takes the three-kernel path: measured 4–36% faster at 1–8 rows on GB10. | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF` | 1 = bulk in the recipe (0/off, 1/bulk, lines, touch; patch: 0) | L2 prefetch in decode (patch 0046, adapted from jayleaton's patch 0460): a side stream brings into L2 the weights the next kernels will read, during each layer's all-gathers. bulk = cp.async.bulk.prefetch.L2; lines = prefetch.global.L2::evict_last; touch = ld.global.cg. | One request: prose 48.36 → 49.46 tok/s, code 59.54 → 61.08 (two boots each). Same bits: the side kernel only reads/prefetches. | 26 Inside the GPU, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF_BLOCKS` | 0 (automatic) | Prefetch kernel blocks per site; 0 = automatic (one thread per piece in bulk, one warp per piece in the other modes). | A fine-tuning knob; the recipe uses the automatic value. | 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF_KB` | 32 (1–1024 KiB) | The largest (address, bytes) piece of the device prefetch table (one bulk prefetch's size, one warp's walk). | A single table of pieces serves every site and is read by the side kernel. | 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF_MB` | 8 (> 0 and ≤ 64 MiB per site) | The prefetch budget per site: small tensors first (scales, biases, norms), then the large matrices' leading bytes. | For a 4-bit matrix whose decode launch fits in one wave (≤ 192 programs = 4 CTAs/SM × 48 SMs on GB10, according to the code), it prefetches the start of each chunk, so every program starts with L2 hits. | 26 Inside the GPU, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF_ROWS` | 64 | Windows with more rows than this (and prompt chunks) skip every prefetch site. | Wide windows and prompts already spend more time in compute than waiting on DRAM (interpretation). | 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF_SITES` | afo | Prefetch sites: a at the attention all-gather (HC weights and FFN norm, router, shared expert); f at the FFN all-gather (the next layer's weights; after the last, the final norm and head); o after the attention's input projections (output projection, kv_b's value half). | With prefetch on, the shared expert runs before the routed ones, so the routed experts' ~100+ MB stream does not evict it from L2. | 26 Inside the GPU, 11 Mixture-of-Experts |
| `TF_GLM_L2PF_THREADS` | 128 (32–1024, whole warps) | Threads per block of the prefetch kernel. | A fine-tuning knob; the recipe uses the default. | 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_LATENT_MMA` | 1 | Latent MLA absorption (_absorb_mma) and expansion (_expand_mma) on prompt rows as tensor-core GEMMs (MMA_TILE = (64, 128, 64), 4 warps, 3 stages); with 0, prompts use the decode kernels. | In prefill there are enough rows to fill the tensor cores; it changes the prompt's bits, not the decode's. | 12 MLA & compact KV, 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_LATENT_STAGES` | 3 (1 = none; 4 does not fit in shared memory) | The Triton pipeline depth of the latent attention's dense and sparse chunk programs. | The loads only, the same bits: it only changes how loads overlap; 4 stages do not fit in shared memory. | 28 Kernels, 26 Inside the GPU |
| `TF_GLM_Q4_TILE` | table (stock, or a number) | The tile of the Q4 decode matmuls (≤ 16 rows): table = the per-shape (n, k) DEC_TILES table measured on GB10; stock = none; a number = that config for every shape (0019 adds configs 12–15, 32 columns wide). | Every output's chain is qmm_kernel's, so every choice gives the same bits: the tile choice is speed only. | 28 Kernels |
| `TF_GLM_SEQ_ROWS` | 256 | From how many rows the BF16/FP8 matmuls take the SEQ path (with n ≥ 512 and k ≤ 4,096), with the per-shape F8_PROMPT tile table. | Measured at 2,048 rows (GB10): 1.3–3.2× on N ≥ 512, K ≤ 4,096; slower on narrow outputs (N 32–288) and long K (6,144, 8,192). No choice changes a row's bits. | 28 Kernels |

#### Launch / topology

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BASE_IMAGE` | nvcr.io/nvidia/pytorch:26.07-py3 | The container's base: CUDA, PyTorch, Triton and the compiler for the CUDA C++ extensions TensorFold JIT-builds on first start. | TensorFold says to use NVIDIA's PyTorch container for CUDA (there is no cuda pip extra). Changing the base needs --rebuild. | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `CONTAINER_NAME` | glm53-flash-tf | The container's name, the same on both Sparks: docker logs -f glm53-flash-tf on the head shows rank 0; over ssh on the worker, rank 1. | start.sh and stop.sh use the name to find, stop and remove each rank; a stopped container from an earlier run is removed before launch. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `FOREGROUND` | 0 (1 = attached to rank 0's log) | 1: stays attached to rank 0's log and exits with its code (for a systemd unit with Restart=on-failure), without progress lines, window retry or smoke test. | If one rank ends, the other is stopped: a lone rank would wait for its peer forever. A watcher checks rank 1 every 30 s. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `GHCR_IMAGE` | ghcr.io/miaai-lab/glm-5.3-flash-exl3-2x-dgx-sparks-tensorfold | The ready image's registry: prepare.sh pulls $GHCR_IMAGE@$IMAGE_DIGEST while the patches are the release's, or $GHCR_IMAGE:v0.6.0-&lt;hash&gt; when changed patches have a published image. | Avoids the local build (~25 GB image). With no image for the hash, no access to the package or no network, prepare.sh builds locally. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `IMAGE` | tensorfold-glm53:${TF_VERSION} (tensorfold-glm53:v0.6.0) | The image's local name on both Sparks; the image pulled from GHCR is re-tagged with it. Its tf.patches label holds the hash of the patches it was built from. | start.sh checks the image on both sides and prepare.sh accepts an image only if its label matches image_hash; otherwise it rebuilds. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `IMAGE_DIGEST` | v1.3.2: sha256:22789f0cb3dc308f0b2ce52a33961b88bd624af1725e91e8aba0a74a671bb969 · now sha256:99fe7496d7002fd8f2ca3b1beb0aa07513835cd09989ca657b65875d60f69956 (4624c21) | The digest of the published v1.3 image (unchanged through v1.3.2): prepare.sh pulls the image by it, not by the tag. | A tag can be moved, a digest cannot: pulling by digest guarantees identical bytes; the image is still accepted only if its tf.patches label matches. | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `IMAGE_EXTRAS` | av==18.1.0 xgrammar>=0.2.8,<0.3 (fixed in config.sh, not read from the environment) | pip packages added on top of TensorFold: PyAV (video input) and xgrammar (response_format and structured outputs). | They enter the image hash together with the patches: changing an extra rebuilds the image as a patch would. | 41 Recipe engineering, 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `IMAGE_TAG` | v1.3.2: v0.6.0-ae8d1c789b47 · now v0.6.0-789e8f3bdfa2 (123 patches, 4624c21) | This release's published tag (&lt;TF_VERSION&gt;-&lt;image_hash&gt;). While the local hash of patches + extras equals this tag's, prepare.sh pulls by IMAGE_DIGEST. | Ties the pinned image to the exact patches; publish-image.sh prints the IMAGE_TAG / IMAGE_DIGEST pair to pin after a push. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `PREPARE` | auto (1 = always, 0 = never) | When start.sh runs scripts/prepare.sh: in auto, when the setup marker differs from the current state. | PREPARE=1 ./start.sh restart forces the setup; with 0 the check is skipped, but the image and snapshots must still exist on both sides. | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `PULL` | 1 (0 = always build locally) | prepare.sh tries the published image first (by the pinned digest or the hash's tag), on the head and on the worker; with 0 it builds. | Pulling needs the network and access to the GHCR package; building locally checks that the patches apply and import on this machine. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `STOP_TIMEOUT` | 30 (s) | Each rank's docker stop -t in stop.sh (and in FOREGROUND). | It does not drain requests: stop.sh reads requests_running from /health and warns how many will be cut off. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `TF_REPO` | https://github.com/ashhart/TensorFold.git | TensorFold's git origin for the image build's pip install. | TensorFold (Ash Hart) is Apache-2.0 from v0.6.0. A fork needs prepare.sh --rebuild and patches that apply cleanly: a failing patch -p0 --forward fails the build. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `TF_VERSION` | v0.6.0 | The TensorFold tag installed in the image (pip install git+TF_REPO@TF_VERSION); it also names the image and the published tag v0.6.0-&lt;hash&gt;. | The patches (53 in v1.3.2; 123 in the image pinned in 4624c21) are diffs against this version's site-packages: changing it needs a rebase of the patches and prepare.sh --rebuild. In v1.3 (v0.5.0 → v0.6.0) three of Mia's patches went upstream and left the list. | 41 Recipe engineering, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `WAIT_TIMEOUT` | 1800 (s) | How long start.sh waits for /v1/models to answer before giving up (both ranks keep running). | An image's first start compiles kernels, and later starts take 2–6 min to load ~80 GiB a Spark. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `WORKER` | empty (required; e.g. user@192.0.2.2) | The worker's ssh target (rank 1). The head (this machine) runs rank 0 and the API; every command on the worker goes through ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10, key-based only, no password. | The worker needs no copy of the repository: the head prepares and launches both ranks. It lives in scripts/local.sh (outside git); without it, need_worker aborts before any step. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 41 Recipe engineering |

With TF's defaults (RAM/10 reserve, 3 GiB): 1,411,072 tokens. On 3 Sparks each holds fewer weights and the default rises to 32 GiB (v1.4); the per-token cost is the same on every rank because | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 27 Unified memory |

Raise it if other work shares the Sparks: on unified memory, running out tends to freeze the machine. Since v1.5 the reserve grows with PARALLEL and with the verify window, because  | 27 Unified memory, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |

On an integrated GPU (GB10) the available memory is MemAvailable − reserve, counting reclaimable page cache. | The reserve also covers the CUDA context, NCCL and workspace that the estimate does not count (TF's GLM doc measured 3.5–4.8 GiB of that). It was a Mia patch that went upstream in v0.6.0. | 27 Unified memory, 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |
| `TF_GLM_CACHE_GIB` | 3 in TF; 12.5 in the recipe (KV_POOL_GIB; 32 at TP3) | In the multi-stream engine: pool_rows = (capacity + max(0, cache_bytes − states) // per_token) // ALIGN · ALIGN, with states = TF_GLM_CACHE_ENTRIES × (slot_bytes(rows=0)/2 + 22 MiB) reserving the kept prompts' fixed state. | Kept prompts now live inside the pool, in extents aligned to 2,048 tokens; the window becomes min(limit, pool_rows − MAX_ROWS). | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 33 Prefix caching |
