#### Operations / Docker

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DFLASH2_REVISION` | bf582e4eacc1810f76656d1811693ff6c6737d2a | DFlash2's revision pin, like MODEL_REVISION: both ranks serve exactly that snapshot from the local cache. | A different drafter does not change replies (drafts only propose), but it changes acceptance and speed: the pin keeps the numbers reproducible (interpretation). | 24 Checkpoints, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `HF_CACHE` | $HF_HOME or ~/.cache/huggingface | The Hugging Face cache on the head, mounted at /root/.cache/huggingface in rank 0; on the worker, its own HF_HOME (copy) or the NFS volume. | The ranks get the snapshot by path (hub/models--…/snapshots/&lt;rev&gt;), and start.sh checks config.json on both sides before launch. | 24 Checkpoints, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` | 1 in the containers (0 lets the server reach the Hub) | Passed to both containers: with 1 the ranks read only the local cache; no Hugging Face token goes into the containers. | Ensures the server serves exactly the pinned snapshots. With 0, huggingface_hub finds the token file in the mounted cache. | 24 Checkpoints, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `HF_TOKEN` | optional (or ~/.cache/huggingface/token) | The Hugging Face token used only by prepare.sh's downloads (gated repositories, rate limits). | It never goes into the serving containers: it is passed only to the throwaway download container when the host has no hf CLI. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `IMAGE_FREE_GB` | 35 | Free disk (GB) under Docker's root to build or copy the image (~25 GB), on each Spark. | Checked on the head before the build or pull and on the worker before the pull or the docker save \| docker load. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `KERNEL_CACHE` | ~/.cache/tensorfold-glm53 (one folder per image hash, mounted at /cache) | Compiled CUDA/Triton kernels (TORCH_EXTENSIONS_DIR=/cache/torch_extensions, TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/cache/triton), in one folder per image patches hash (tf.patches label). | An extension is found by its name, so another image's build of the same name must never be the one loaded. Each image's first start compiles (a few minutes). | 41 Recipe engineering, 28 Kernels |
| `MIN_FREE_GB` | 180 | Free disk (GB) required under HF_CACHE before downloading the checkpoint (~176 GB). | Added to IMAGE_FREE_GB when the cache and Docker's root share a filesystem; on the worker the copy is checked against the snapshot's actual size (+5 GB). | 24 Checkpoints, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `MODEL_ID` | v1.3.2: Mia-AiLab/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-TR3-4bpw · now Mia-AiLab/GLM-5.3-Flash-EXL3-4bpw-TensorFold (v1.3.3); with ABLIT=1, …-TensorFold-Ablit | The checkpoint served: routed experts in EXL3 at 4 bits per weight, BF16 elsewhere; ~176 GB (164 GiB). In v1.3.2, a byte-identical mirror of brandonmusic/GLM-5.3-Flash-tr3-4bpw, the same checkpoint as the vLLM recipe at the time. Since v1.3.3 (2026-10-02) it is Mia's own EXL3 qua | In v1.3.2 the recipe changed the engine, not the model. Against Z.AI's official FP8, the new checkpoint measured 2–18% lower KL than TR3 on all six test sets (paired 95% intervals exclude zero on all but chat), with equal coding (HumanEval+ and MBPP+, thinking on: 469 vs 468 of 542). Another MODEL_ID gets no revision pin (only the default | 24 Checkpoints, 22 Hadamard, trellises & EXL3 |
| `MODEL_REVISION` | v1.3.2: 9eaebb7c4e96d983dcd538e18624622ba5b820a8 · now 078455ffe6472f9a52fbc1139f58b9db2881b25c (Ablit: 57edefd2…); only for the default MODEL_IDs | The Hugging Face commit pin: prepare.sh downloads exactly this revision and both ranks serve that snapshot from the local cache (HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1). Empty = the Hub's main at first download. | Reproducibility: a new upstream commit changes nothing until the pin does. A download by sha writes no refs/main; prepare.sh writes it for tools that take repo ids. | 24 Checkpoints, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `NCCL_DEBUG` | empty (passed on only when set) | NCCL's log level, passed to both containers when set. | Diagnoses the link (HCAs, GID, channels) when startup fails in the collectives. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NFS_PATH` | $HF_CACHE | The exported path as the worker mounts it in the NFS volume (device=:$NFS_PATH). | For an NFSv4 export with fsid=0, use /. If the existing volume points to another path or server, prepare.sh stops and asks to remove it. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `NFS_SERVER` | empty = the head's address on the link (HEAD_ADDR) | The NFS server of the worker's volume (addr= in the mount options). | By default it is the head's address on the CX7 link, so weight reads use the same link as the ranks (interpretation). | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `NFS_VOLUME` | glm53-hf | The NFS docker volume on the worker: type=nfs, nfsvers=4.2,ro,nconnect=8,rsize=1048576,wsize=1048576,hard,timeo=600; mounted read-only in rank 1. | A docker volume mounts NFS without sudo on the worker; prepare.sh checks that the worker sees every file of both snapshots with the same size as the head. | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 27 Unified memory |
| `RSYNC_OPTS` | empty | Extra options for the rsync -a -L --partial that copies the blobs from the head to the worker. | The copy resumes if interrupted and is then checked file by file (path + size). | 24 Checkpoints |
| `STATE_DIR` | ~/.local/state/glm53-tensorfold | Locks (flock: one start.sh and one prepare.sh at a time) and the setup marker prepared. | start.sh runs prepare.sh whenever the current prepared_state line (model@revision, drafter@revision, image on both sides, patches hash, worker, weights mode) differs from the recorded one. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `TENSORFOLD_NO_LIVE` | not set | Turns off the live throughput line TensorFold writes to the terminal. | An engine knob the recipe does not set; useful when the log goes to a file or to systemd (interpretation). | 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |
| `TENSORFOLD_NO_UPDATE_CHECK` | 1 | Turns off TensorFold's new-release check at startup. | Offline, reproducible serving: the containers run with HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 and nothing at startup should depend on the network. | 41 Recipe engineering |
| `TF_GLM_EXIT_ON_BROKEN` | 0; 1 exits with code 1 (patch 0118) | When the concurrent GLM decoder latches an error that leaves the ranks out of step, with 1 the process logs the first cause and exits at once with code 1, so a supervisor can restart both ranks; independent of the stall watchdog. With 0 requests keep failing, but /health shows ok | After an error the ranks can no longer agree: broken latches and every call fails until both restart; before 0118 this did not show in the health check. A GPU fault may still need host recovery; use restart backoff. On clusters this is an orchestrator's liveness contract (Kubernetes, Slurm): the process dies fast and the supervisor decide | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |
| `TF_GLM_MULTI_WATCHDOG_S` | 300 (0 turns it off) | If a rank-0 iteration (or rank 1's wait for a message) takes longer than this, every thread's Python stack goes to the log (faulthandler). | Diagnoses hangs between ranks: collectives that do not match wait forever. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 48 Exact engine (TensorFold) |
| `WORKER_WEIGHTS` | copy (or nfs) | copy: the worker keeps its own copy of the checkpoint and DFlash2 (~166 GiB copied over the link with rsync); nfs: rank 1 reads the head's HF_CACHE over NFS, read-only, through a docker volume created without sudo. | NFS saves ~166 GiB of disk on the worker and loads as fast as from the local disk (both ranks live in ~2.2 min). The head has to export the cache once, as root. | 27 Unified memory, 24 Checkpoints |

| `PREFIX_WAIT` | 0 (off); 1 turns it on (→ TF_GLM_PREFIX_WAIT, patch 0128) | Single-flight prefix prefill: a request whose prompt starts with a point another request is still prefilling (the end of the system block, a shared-prefix point) waits for that state, apart from the queue, and resumes from it instead of prefilling the same prefix beside it. | Six agents at once on a fresh 12k-token system prompt, 2 Sparks, PARALLEL=8, every reply equal to its own fresh draft: false one: all answered in 47.0 / 45.7 s → 14.7 / 15.0 s; slowest first token 46.2 / 45.0 → 13.5 / 13.8 s; 60,160 → 0 system-prompt tokens prefilled again. Off by default because it changes admission (also with SPILL_GIB= | 33 Prefix caching, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `SHARED_PREFIX` | 1 | Conversations that share a system prompt reuse its prompt state (patch 0015; → TF_GLM_SHARED_PREFIX). | A 7.9k-token system prompt: the second and later chats prefill in 0.13 s instead of 4.24 s. Same replies. | 33 Prefix caching, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `SPILL_GIB / SPILL_DIR` | 0 (off) / ~/.cache/tensorfold-spill; e.g. SPILL_GIB=64 | An NVMe spill tier for kept prompt states: with PARALLEL above 1, every kept state of ≥ SPILL_MIN_TOKENS (8,192) is written to each Spark's local disk as soon as it is kept, in 2,048-token blocks named by a hash chained over the token ids and the lineage (later turns and forks re | Without the tier, a conversation that leaves the pool (and every one after a restart) pays a full prefill (~2 min at 200k tokens). 2 Sparks, PARALLEL=8, SPILL_GIB=64, replies equal to a fresh draft: false one: a 40k conversation resumed from disk after kill -9 in 1.4 s (fresh 21.8 s); restoring a 40k state, 147 ms (median); a 260k one bes | 33 Prefix caching, 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold |
| `TF_GLM_CACHE_ENTRIES` | 32 (TF: 8) | Maximum kept prompt states. In v1.3.2, past this count the oldest went, however much of the pool was free. Since then the order changed: a conversation's superseded states go first (0063), shared prefixes by recency (0077), a state another conversation went on from stays as a pre | A drafted agent request keeps 1 to 3 (its own + shared-prefix states), so 8 let three or four alternating conversations push each other out (#17). 32 took ~1 GiB more than 8 (at ~45 MiB an entry). | 33 Prefix caching, 31 PagedAttention & KV |
| `TF_GLM_KEPT_BYTES_GIB / TF_GLM_KEEP_PER_CHAT` | 0 / 0 (no limit); the author runs 4 / 2 | Two optional limits on the kept prompt states (0089): TF_GLM_KEPT_BYTES_GIB caps the device memory they own outside the pool (recurrent state, conv windows, DFlash2 window copies); past the cap the least valuable state goes and freed blocks return to the driver. TF_GLM_KEEP_PER_C | Nothing hands that memory back by itself: on a long multi-agent session MemAvailable falls and stays down (issue #197). One user's soak (v1.10, 2 Sparks, PARALLEL=2, three agents growing to 400K tokens, first hour): with TF_GLM_KEPT_BYTES_GIB=1, kept_bytes 1.0 GiB (2.8 uncapped) and ~4 GiB more MemAvailable on both Sparks (head 7.6–8.4 ag | 33 Prefix caching, 27 Unified memory |
| `TF_GLM_PROMPT_GRID` | 0 in the patch; 64 with TF_GLM_KDA_CHUNKED=1 (16, 32, 64, 128) | Grid G: every prompt chunk starts on a multiple of G and the kept state sits at the prompt's last multiple of G (grid_point); the request prefills up to there, takes the snapshot and resumes from it. | Kernels whose bits depend on block alignment (the chunked KDA) resume exactly: any prompt that extends prompt[:B] passes through the same state, fresh or resumed. TF_GLM_PREFILL_ROWS must be a multiple of G. | 33 Prefix caching, 14 Linear attention & hybrids |
| `TF_GLM_SHARED_PREFIX` | 0 in the patch; 1/on = 256 tokens; or ≥ 16 tokens | A drafted text request also keeps states where its prompt stops sharing with a kept prompt and at the end of its system block (first &lt;\|user\|&gt;), each on the grid and only if it adds ≥ N tokens; at most 2 per request. | Fresh and resumed pass through the same states; saved states carry DFlash2's context window to serve the drafter. Prompts with images are not kept. | 33 Prefix caching, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |

#### Quantization

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DENSE` | q4 (fp8, bf16) | The checkpoint's BF16 weights outside the routed experts (attention, shared experts, dense layers, head: ~9.7 GiB a Spark; → TF_GLM_DENSE): q4 = affine 4-bit groups of 64 with MSE-searched ranges, head in FP8, kv_b in BF16; fp8 = e4m3 per row and 128-column block; bf16 = as is. | q4 over fp8 (DFlash2, one boot each): prose 38.9 → 44.4 tok/s, code 44.2 → 48.7, prefill ~1,090 → ~1,260 tok/s; GSM8K 98.0% and HumanEval 95.1% on both (TR3 checkpoint) (bf16: 97.2 / 96.3); fp8 gives ~+33% decode over bf16. But q4 loses the end of turn on short French coding prompts (12/48 vs 1/48, #18): for non-English prompts, fp8, ~10% | 20 Quantization fundamentals, 25 Measuring quality |
| `TF_GLM_DENSE` | bf16 in the patch; q4 in the recipe (DENSE) | The engine switch: fp8 quantizes at load with an fp32 scale = absmax/448 per row × 128-column block (K not a multiple of 128 stays BF16); q4 (quantize4_mse) tries 16 clippings of the range (1.0 to 0.55 of min/max) per group of 64 and keeps the one with the least squared error. | The kernels (Triton _fmm, TF's Q4) keep rows independent and split-K fixed by shape: lossy against the checkpoint, but drafted = serial. q4 costs ~4.5 effective bits per weight (bf16 scale + bias per 64 values) [estimate]. | 20 Quantization fundamentals, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
| `TF_GLM_DRAFT_QUANT` | minmax (or mse) | How the draft-only 4-bit copies (DFlash2's matrices and an EXL3 checkpoint's draft head) are made: minmax (each group's full min/max range) or mse (least-squares clipping). | Drafts only propose, so either keeps every reply; only acceptance can move. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 20 Quantization fundamentals |
| `TF_GLM_KVB` | bf16 (fp8, q4); the recipe does not set it → bf16 | The format of the latent path's kv_b_proj (which runs once a DSA layer every round): bf16 as stored, fp8 (e4m3 per head, a scale per row and 128 latent columns) or q4 (MSE affine groups of 64). | fp8 and q4 are lossy like the rest of TF_GLM_DENSE; with DENSE=q4 kv_b stays BF16 (the docs said FP8, fixed in v1.3.2). The kv_k/kv_v copies only exist with TF_GLM_LATENT=0. | 12 MLA & compact KV, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |

#### Reasoning / tools

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MAX_TOKENS` | 32768 (TF: 4096) | Reply budget (reasoning + answer) of a request with neither max_tokens nor max_completion_tokens (--max-tokens); cut to what the window has left, never refused. | GLM thinks at Max by default and TF's 4,096 could end a reply inside a tool call (agents such as Codex send no max_tokens). A request's own value always wins. | 34 API, tools & JSON, 09 Generation & sampling |
| `TF_GLM_KEEP_REASONING` | 1024 (replies; 0 turns it off) | An LRU of the reasoning of replies that made tool calls, keyed by the call ids and by a signature (sha256 of the whole conversation before the calls + the calls); restore() puts reasoning_content back on the steps that came back without it. | GLM-5.3's template renders each step's reasoning; a client that drops it produces a context the model never wrote and a prompt that stops matching the previous one, and the prefix cache re-reads the rest. | 34 API, tools & JSON, 33 Prefix caching |
| `TF_GLM_KEEP_REASONING_MB` | 32 (MiB of text) | The cap on text kept by the reasoning LRU. | A reasoning larger than the cap is not kept; the oldest go first. | 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `THINKING` | 1 | --thinking / --no-thinking: think before answering by default. | With 0 the model answers directly unless a request asks (reasoning_effort or enable_thinking). Without an explicit effort, GLM's template thinks at Max. | 09 Generation & sampling, 34 API, tools & JSON |

Issue #91: the stock test failed whenever two ranks both held part of the nucleus and sent almost every sampled step (temperature 1, top_p 0.95) to a whole-vocabulary CPU sort: ~15 tok/s against greedy's ~58 on 2 Sparks; wit | 09 Generation & sampling, 38 Parallelism |

With the sliced fill (0062, FILL_BUDGET_MS) that wait became time-bounded, and the recipe uses the whole 2,048-row chunk, whose buffers already exist: the same memory, the same replies, half the passes over the weights. 3 Sparks, PARAL | 32 Scheduler & batching |

| `COPY` | 1 | Copy drafts (prompt lookup, → TF_GLM_COPY_DRAFTS): when the last 8 tokens occurred before, the tokens that followed them are verified ahead of DFlash2's. | Quote / edit replies +5% (80.3 → 84.4 tok/s), prose and code unchanged. | `COPY_CODE` | 1 (→ TF_GLM_WIDE_GRAPHS=16, TF_GLM_COPY_REPLY_MATCH=16; 0 → 0/0) | Code-workload copy drafts (patch 0032): 16-row verify windows as CUDA graphs, and a copy from the reply itself only when its last 16 tokens match. | Code 55.4 → 56.2 tok/s, edit 120 → 125 (with SPLIT's overlap, two boots each). Exact. Over 39 code replies, copies from the reply itself kept 23% of their drafts and copies from the prompt 74%. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `COPY_MAX` | 15 (1–15) | Copy drafts a round (→ TF_GLM_COPY_MAX); possible because patch 0013 widened the verify window to 16 rows (the pending token + 15). | 15 lets a long quote go through in one round: edit replies 84 → 119 tok/s, prose and code unchanged (two boots each). Exact. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `DFLASH2_ID` | incoai/GLM-5.3-Flash-DFlash2 | The DFlash2 drafter (~2.3 GB) trained by IncoAI on the z-lab/dflash architecture; downloaded only with DRAFTER=dflash2. | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 license (non-commercial). In the engine the draft model takes 0.63 GiB a rank (4-bit copies + the selector's codebooks). Another id has no pin and needs prepare.sh --rebuild. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 24 Checkpoints |
| `DRAFT_POLICY` | fnc7:0.3 | How many DFlash2 drafts a round verifies (→ TF_GLM_DFLASH_POLICY): up to 7, stopping when the product of each draft's chance under the request's own keyed sampling noise drops below 0.3 (patch 0018). | Prose 47.2 → 50.6 tok/s, code 51.7 → 55.5 over fc5:0.3 (two boots each); replies are the same under every policy. start.sh requires a DFlash2 policy (f prefix: fc5:0.3, fnc7:0.3, fcost7:noisy…). | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `DRAFTER` | dflash2 (or mtp) | dflash2: drafts from incoai/GLM-5.3-Flash-DFlash2 (--drafter &lt;snapshot&gt;); mtp: the checkpoint's own MTP head (--drafter none), one request at a time. | DFlash2 gives +5–10% decode over mtp and is the only one that allows PARALLEL > 1, but it is CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 (non-commercial use only). Setting mtp before the first start avoids downloading DFlash2. The reply is the same with either drafter. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `TF_GLM_COPY_DRAFTS` | 0 in the patch; 1 in the recipe (COPY) | Turns on the CopyDrafts index (numpy, per request): it finds the most recent earlier occurrence of the suffix and proposes what followed; the proposal replaces the drafter's chain for that round. | Both ranks hold the same prompt and tokens, so they compute the same proposals with no exchange; the setting joins the startup comparison. DFlash2's policy does not learn from copied rounds. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `TF_GLM_COPY_MATCH` | 8 (2–64) | How many of the last tokens (including the pending one) must have occurred before, in the prompt or the reply, to propose a copy. | A long enough suffix makes the continuation predictable without any model; pure numpy, identical on both ranks. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `TF_GLM_COPY_MAX` | min(5, max drafts) in the patch; 15 in the recipe (COPY_MAX) | The engine's ceiling on copies a round (1 up to the window's maximum drafts). | 0007 fixed 5 because the 6-row window fit the CUDA graphs of the time; with MAX_ROWS = 16 (0013) 15 fit, and 9–16-row windows only become graphs with TF_GLM_WIDE_GRAPHS (0032). | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `TF_GLM_COPY_MISS_MAX` | 0 (off; up to TF_GLM_COPY_MAX) | After a copy round that did not keep all its drafts, the next one proposes at most this many, until a copy round gets everything right again. | Bounds the cost of wrong copies (a missed round costs a wide window and the DFlash2 round it displaced); the recipe leaves it off. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `TF_GLM_COPY_REPLY_MATCH` | 0 in the patch (off); 16 in the recipe (COPY_CODE); 0 or > TF_GLM_COPY_MATCH, up to 64 | An occurrence that starts inside the reply itself must match the last N tokens (not just TF_GLM_COPY_MATCH's 8) to become a copy. | Code repeats short snippets (def __init__(self, });) with different continuations: copies from the reply kept 23% of drafts against 74% from the prompt, and a missed copy round costs a wide window and the DFlash2 round it displaced. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `TF_GLM_DFLASH_POLICY` | fc5:0.3 in the patch; fnc7:0.3 in the recipe (DRAFT_POLICY) | The DFlash2 policy for requests that ask for no other (and for auto on an EXL3 checkpoint with a drafter); validated at boot as a DFlash2 policy (f…, kind ≥ 10). | Under --parallel, auto becomes this policy and an MTP policy becomes its DFlash2 twin: drafts never change a reply. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `TF_GLM_DRAFT_DUMP` | empty (off); a directory, on both ranks | Requests that would draft with DFlash2 decode one token a round (reply = serial) and record, per position, candidates, logits, selector edges, the keyed Gumbel draws and the committed token (one .npz per request, on rank 0). | Since sampling is keyed by (seed, position, token), the draft_sim simulator (numpy, CPU) replays any policy exactly offline; that is how the stop rules were tuned (β = 2.0 over 9 dumps). | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `TF_GLM_DRAFT_RING` | 1 (0 = a buffer of the whole window) | DFlash2's context in a 2,176-row ring (the 2,048 sliding window + a block + a tile): 21 MiB a rank whatever the window. | It used to be 10 KiB a rank per window token; 8% faster at 52k, and 3.8 GiB lighter a rank on two Sparks. With 0, the same drafts. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 33 Prefix caching |
| `TF_GLM_MAX_ROWS` | 16 (16–64, in steps of 8) | The widest verify window per stream. | Past 16 rows the window's all-gathers exceed 256 KiB (16 KiB per row) and go over NCCL, unless TF_ROCE_MAX_KB goes up (512 for 32 rows). | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TF_GLM_MTP` | auto (TF v0.6.0: 1) | Load the checkpoint's MTP head beside DFlash2? auto leaves it out while DFlash2 drafts every request; 1 loads it (1.77 GiB a Spark) with PARALLEL=1; 0 never. | It was a Mia patch that went upstream in v0.6.0, but with default 1; the recipe now sets auto so as not to pay 1.77 GiB a Spark. With 1, the auto policy compares the drafters on greedy requests. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `TF_GLM_MULTI_DEPTH` | policy (joint, scale:A) | Depth under concurrency: policy = each stream as if alone; joint = greedily gives the round's rows to the draft with the highest reach while expected tokens per ms grow (a curve measured at startup); scale:A = threshold min(0.95, p·S^A) with S streams. | With a shared window, the per-stream optimal depth is not the global optimum; no mode changes the reply, and only policy is used in the recipe (no published number for the others). | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 32 Scheduler & batching |
| `TF_GLM_MULTI_DRAFT_GRAPHS` | 1 | The multi-stream drafter's CUDA graphs: one per stream count (1…streams) for the block pass and one per row bucket for the context updates. | Capture uses tables that send everything to "trash" rows, so it touches no live stream; with 0 it runs eagerly. | 37 DFlash2 & DSpark, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `TF_GLM_MULTI_OVERHEAD_MS` | 12 (0–1000) | The rest of the round (ms) added to the batched window's measured curve in the joint allocation. | Models a round's fixed cost beyond the verify forward. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `TF_GLM_MULTI_VERIFY` | batched (or serial) | batched: one verify forward over all streams' rows (KDA through the segmented chain, DSA through dsa_segments); serial: each window as its own forward, the reference. | The serial reference makes it possible to check bit for bit that concurrent = solo. | 35 Speculation: fundamentals, 32 Scheduler & batching |
| `TF_GLM_MULTI_WINDOW` | 32; 64 with PARALLEL > 4 (16–64, step 8; patch 0069, v1.5) | Rows of every request's verify window together in one round (--parallel). With more requests each one's drafts get fewer of these rows (8 at 32: ~3 drafts each). Past 32 rows a round's all-gathers exceed 512 KiB (16 KiB a row), so TF_ROCE_MAX_KB rises with it (64 rows: 1024) unle | At 8 requests, code 167.0 / 160.3 / 141.2 tok/s at 64 / 48 / 32 rows; prose the same at any size (2 Sparks, sparkDash). Exact at any size; 64 rows add ~250 MiB a Spark to the estimate and ~0.04 GiB a row past 32 to the reserve. It is the per-step verification token budget any server doing batched speculation has to split among requests (i | 49 GLM recipe · TensorFold, 37 DFlash2 & DSpark |
| `TF_GLM_WIDE_GRAPHS` | empty/0 in the patch (wide windows run eagerly); 16 in the recipe (COPY_CODE) | Window widths past 8 rows (9 up to TF_GLM_MAX_ROWS) that also replay CUDA graphs, comma separated (e.g. 16 or 16,32). Copy drafts pad a short window by repeating the last draft up to the captured width. | Each width costs at startup one graph per KDA parity, and one more per pool bucket past the dense limit. In multi-stream mode there is no padding (batched windows are not a single stream's widths). | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |

## Qwen3.8-Flash-Next NVFP4

### Modifications (patches, overlays, kernels, hotfixes, checkpoint transforms)

#### Attention / kernels

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_qsa_mqa_paged_kernel` | Triton scoring kernel of the QSA indexer (patched for FP8) | Scores compressed keys (compress ratio 4, 128 dims) over the paged KV to select the top-k of ~2048 tokens. | Quantizing K changes the scores and can change which blocks are chosen — a quality risk of FP8 KV. | 13 Sparse attention, 28 Kernels |
| `FlashAttentionImpl.__init__ recebe 'auto' (neutraliza a recusa do pai)` | restores the real dtype after __init__ | The QSA backend hands 'auto' to FlashAttentionImpl.__init__ and restores the real dtype right after; it also relaxes the other two dtype guards. | FlashAttention refuses quantized KV. This is safe because the layer's only entry point is forward_qsa, which uses the Triton kernels (FlashAttention is only a nominal base class). | 30 vLLM architecture, 23 Quantized KV cache |
| `persistent_topk` | launched over page_table_width×page_size columns | QSA top-k kernel that picks the tokens each query attends to. | At 1M and compress ratio 4 it runs over 250K columns per layer even on short prompts — one of the costs of a high MAX_MODEL_LEN. | 13 Sparse attention, 28 Kernels |

| `bench/longctx.py` | --target 600000, --max-tokens 1024; needles at 5%/50%/95% | Measures prefill tok/s = prompt_tokens/TTFT and needle PASS/FAIL at long context. | A UUID salt at the start kills prefix-cache reuse; it calibrates with /tokenize (~25 tokens/line). | 45 Benchmarking, 25 Measuring quality |
| `bench/mtp_accept.py` | --save, --since, --url · reads spec_decode_num_accepted_tokens_per_pos_total from /metrics | Computes the delta of the spec-decode counters between two points in time. · Measures per-position MTP acceptance from /metrics, always as a delta between reads. | The cumulative value mixes all clients; the per-position curve should decay (e.g., 0.81/0.64/0.51 on prose). · A curve that decreases by position (89/74.5/60%) is the healthy signal; a flat or random curve points to misaligned scales. | 45 Benchmarking, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `bench/reasoning_check.py` | 10 deterministic tasks + --needles 8000,64000, --max-tokens 2048, T=0, --compare · deterministic tasks + needles (13k and 104k) | X/12 reasoning + needles score; --compare exits with a nonzero code on regression. · Functional quality test used to validate FP8 KV. | Created because FP8 KV's failure mode is a wrong answer, not slowness; fp8 vs bf16 gave 12/12 vs 12/12. · 12/12 on bf16 and fp8 (2026-09-05); needed because sparse attention may select different blocks with FP8 KV. | 25 Measuring quality, 45 Benchmarking |
| `bench/sweep.py` | --tag, --streams 1 2 4, --prompt prose, --repeats 3, --max-tokens 600, --out logs/sweep.jsonl; via sparkDash (:5555) · S=1/2/4/8, 3 alternating repeats, one launch per arm | Concurrency sweep that measures ms/step = Δ(ITL_sum)/Δ(count), tok/step per position, minimum MemAvailable/MemFree and NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY from journalctl. · Throughput sweep along a concurrency ladder. | Alternating order per repeat and retry on 409/429; individual cells do not reproduce (stdev 2–5%), the mean does. · Re-measure after changing MAX_MODEL_LEN, MTP_NUM_SPECULATIVE_TOKENS, KV_CACHE_DTYPE, MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB, FP8_DENSE or QSA_PROFILE; sparkDash's 'code' prompt is synthetic (acceptance ~1.0). | 45 Benchmarking |
| `files/qsa_gb10/bench_qsa_kernels.py` | --tp 2, --rows 1 4 8 32, --ctx 4096 32768 131072, --out qsa_profile.json | Microbenchmark of the QSA kernels: sweeps block_n∈{16,32,64} × splits∈{1..64} × warps∈{2,4} and MQA tiles∈{1..16} × warps∈{1,2,4} (page 1600, top-k 2048+3, 12 q-heads × 1 kv-head × 256 on TP2). | Generates the table for VLLM_QSA_PROFILE_JSON; not run (GPU busy). | 28 Kernels, 45 Benchmarking |
| `nccl-tests all_reduce_perf` | -b 4K -e 64M -f 2 -g 1 (suggested) | Measures all-reduce latency/bandwidth isolated from vLLM, with NCCL_DEBUG=INFO. | 8–32 KB latency is what matters for decode; check GDRDMA/dmabuf. A fabric failure shows up as a silent hang during vLLM init. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 45 Benchmarking |
| `vllm bench serve` | --dataset-name random --random-input-len 512 --random-output-len 512 --num-prompts 8 --max-concurrency 1 --temperature 0 --seed 0 --percentile-metrics ttft,tpot,itl (suggested) | vLLM's official benchmark for batch-1 decode. | Gives TTFT/TPOT/ITL comparable with other recipes. | 45 Benchmarking |

#### KV cache

| `_qsa_scale_ptr` | 0-d fp32 scale; 1.0 cached per device when absent | Helper that hands the kernel the pointer to the K/V scale, creating a 1.0 per device when the layer has no scale. | Allows running FP8 KV without calibrated scales in the checkpoint. | 23 Quantized KV cache |
| `files/patch_qsa_fp8_kv.py` | → files/qsa_ops_patched.py (nvidia/ops/qsa.py) and files/qsa_nvidia_patched.py (nvidia/qsa.py); automatic if KV_CACHE_DTYPE=fp8* | The Triton kernels _qsa_mqa_paged_kernel and sparse GQA split-K gain k_scale_ptr/v_scale and a KV_QUANT_MODE constexpr; the uint8 cache becomes float8_e4m3fn without a copy (_qsa_as_fp8), the per-tensor scale is applied after the tensor-core dot (scores *= k_scale), and the suppo | The stock backend only accepts auto/bfloat16 (requires a BF16 main KV cache). Gain: 3,652,200 vs 2,131,159 tokens (1.70×/GiB, not 2× because the QSA indexer/compressor and the GDN state stay in BF16), 12/12 reasoning+needle, −5.3% throughput; inert with BF16. Upstream warning: a long-reasoning benchmark dropped from 6/6 to 2/6 in the refe | 23 Quantized KV cache, 28 Kernels |

| `patch_qsa_fp8_kv.py` | mounted when KV_CACHE_DTYPE=fp8* (default) | Enables FP8 KV in the QSA Triton kernels on the day-0 image (nvidia/ops/qsa.py and nvidia/qsa.py); approach credited to lancelind/qwen3.8-Flash-DGX. | 2,131,159 → 3,652,200 tokens (×1.70), concurrency at 262k 8.13× → 13.93×, reasoning_check 12/12, −5.3% average throughput. 'Delete this file ... once the image moves to vLLM 0.31'. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 41 Recipe engineering |

Probably fails on a clean clone (qsa.py.orig is in .gitignore). | 28 Kernels, 26 Inside the GPU |

| `start-tp1.sh` | [--no-launch]; TP1 defaults override the 2-node .env | Single-Spark TP=1 lane with a smaller checkpoint, mmap PLE offload, a derived memory budget and host guards. | On a single Spark the nvidia checkpoint (133 GB) does not fit; local-inference-lab (98.6 GiB) only fits with the PLE off the GPU and almost no headroom. | 27 Unified memory, 43 Qwen recipe |

#### Memory / weight loading

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_attach_packed_table` | np.memmap(mode='r') → torch.from_numpy | If VLLM_PLE_PACKED_TABLE_DIR holds the packed table, validates width, rows and size, maps the file without a copy and frees the anonymous allocations; the PLE shards are not even read. | File-backed pages are evictable: the 26.8 GiB NVFP4 table stays in the page cache instead of anonymous RSS. | 27 Unified memory |
| `_offload_quant_method` | assigned on the layer and injected into the GPU placeholder | Now assigns the attribute, which was read but never written, and injects it into the GPU placeholder from the config. | The NVFP4 dequant branch under offload was dead code; the placeholder did not know it was NVFP4 because the subclass constructor is skipped (PleOffloadLayer.__init_subclass__). | 27 Unified memory, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `build_ple_packed_table.py` | output [total_rows, 90] in {vname}.packed_u8 + .json | Reads the safetensors headers, memmaps the 128 shards and concatenates codes and scales per row in chunks of 2^19 rows; renames the model.language_model. prefix → language_model.model. | ~40 s and less than 1 GiB of RAM to generate the 26.8 GiB (≈320M × 90 B), ready for mmap. | 24 Checkpoints, 27 Unified memory |
| `connector.py (_wait_done)` | 2000 active spins, then 50 µs sleeps until flag ≥ seq | Removes the request thread: the model thread sends over a ZMQ PUSH socket and blocks on _wait_done(seq), with timeout VLLM_PLE_OFFLOAD_STEP_TIMEOUT. | Correct synchronization without stream memory ops; dummy runs only zero the buffers and release_outputs becomes a no-op. | 27 Unified memory |
| `echo 3 \| sudo tee /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches` | sync first; requires root | Drops the host's entire page cache. | Complements the selective eviction when something else filled the cache before the launch. | 27 Unified memory |
| `evict_page_cache.py` | EVICT_PAGE_CACHE=true (default) | Calls os.posix_fadvise(fd, 0, 0, POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED) on the checkpoint's .safetensors/.bin/.pt/.gguf files: clean pages only, no root, never fails the launch; in NFS mode it runs in a throwaway container with the same volume. | On GB10 the page cache shares the unified pool with the model and caused CUDA OOM at load (issue #35): 4.15 → 0.00 GiB resident per shard; NFS 4.2 3.00 → 0.00 GiB. | 27 Unified memory |
| `fast path de largura empacotada (_packed_table)` | prefers the _packed_table mmap; otherwise builds [codes \| scales] | Generalizes the offload fast path to the packed row width. | Previously, the reshape to head_dim made torch.index_select(out=...) silently resize the output (allocating a new tensor instead of writing into the shared buffer) and the GPU waited forever ('fluent gibberish, no error'). | 27 Unified memory |
| `files/build_ple_packed_table.py` | [total_rows, 90] uint8 (codes + scales) | Packs the NVFP4 PLE table into a single flat, mmap-able file. | A table in page cache is evictable; copied into anonymous RAM it would occupy non-evictable memory. | 27 Unified memory, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `files/evict_page_cache.py` | posix_fadvise(fd,0,0,POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED); read-only, clean pages only | Evicts the pages of the checkpoint's heavy files from the page cache, without root. | With UMA the page cache steals memory from CUDA: 4.15 → 0.00 GiB (local) and 3.00 → 0.00 GiB (NFS), measured via mincore. | 27 Unified memory |
| `files/memwatch.sh` | poll 1 s; timeline 5 s; floor MEMWATCH_MIN_GIB | Watchdog that runs docker kill when MemAvailable drops below the floor and records the cgroup's memory.current. | With UMA an exhausted pool hangs the kernel instead of raising OOM; this is a second line of defense, since a userspace poller alone cannot catch GiB/s collapses. | 27 Unified memory |
| `files/patch_ple_offload.py` | → files/ple_offload/{protocol,connector,worker,ple_offload_layer}.py (TP1); requires the mp executor | Replaces synchronization via stream memory ops with a host-side handshake that uses a sequence number in a shared-memory flag; stream-mem-ops become no-ops and the NVFP4 PLE table (26.8 GiB) is mmapped from a flat file. | GB10 reports CU_DEVICE_ATTRIBUTE_CAN_USE_STREAM_MEM_OPS = 0: cuStreamWaitValue32 returns success but blocks the host, and nothing waits inside a CUDA graph — the worker hung after capture. TP1 works with ~77–78 GiB non-evictable + KV instead of ~104 GiB + KV. | 27 Unified memory, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `get_offload_output_dtype (restaurado)` | uint8 (NVFP4) / float8_e4m3fn (FP8) | Restores the function, which had been deleted because it appeared only as an anchor; it defines the dtype of the offload's IPC buffer. | Without it, index_select(... out=buffer) fails with 'self and result must have the same scalar type'. | 27 Unified memory, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `memwatch.sh` | memwatch.sh {container} {min_gib}; default floor 6 GiB | Reads MemAvailable every 1 s and runs docker kill on the container when it drops below the floor, logging every 5 s. | On GB10, exhausting unified memory hangs the kernel with no OOM and no logs; it is the second line of defense behind the cgroup (used on TP1). | 27 Unified memory |
| `patch_ple_offload.py` | TP1 only (start-tp1.sh / tp1/start.sh) | Replaces the PLE offload's synchronization via stream memory ops with a host-side handshake based on a sequence number, and adds the mmap table. In stock, a PleOffloadWorker builds the model on the meta device and materializes only the PLE on CPU. | On GB10 the GPU worker spun forever after graph capture. With the patch, TP1 started serving, going from ~104 → ~77 GiB non-evictable; 'The PLE layer sits at layer 1, so the lost CPU/GPU overlap is negligible'. | 27 Unified memory, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `placeholder GPU da PLE (correções GB10)` | repeatable load_weights; IPC buffer trimmed to 16×90 = 1440 B | Accepts load_weights being called multiple times (the global scale may arrive later) and trims the IPC buffer from 2560 to the 1440 valid bytes. | Required for the placeholder under offload to serve NVFP4 correctly on TP1. | 27 Unified memory |
| `ple_offload_layer.py (reset/signal/wait_reset/ple_offload_wait → no-ops)` | no-ops | reset, signal, wait_reset and the ple_offload_wait custom op become no-ops. | Removes the wait via cuStreamWaitValue32, which does not work on GB10. | 27 Unified memory, 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `protocol.py (done_flag / seq)` | int64 done_flag in shared memory | Adds PleOffloadRegistration.done_flag and PleOffloadRequest.seq to the offload protocol. | It is the foundation of the host-side sequence-number handshake. | 27 Unified memory |
| `worker.py (copy_stream.synchronize + flag[0] = seq)` | sequence-number signaling | After the async H2D copy into each TP rank's GPU buffer, synchronizes all copy_streams and writes flag[0] = seq. | Tells the GPU side that the PLE result is ready, without relying on stream memory ops. | 27 Unified memory |

#### MoE

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Fp8MoEMethod` | weight_block_size=[128,128]; weight_scale_inv scales | Existing vLLM FP8 MoE method, which names the scales weight_scale_inv. | vLLM already had the method, but the ModelOpt dispatch did not call it for the MTP's block-wise FP8 experts. | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `patch_modelopt_fp8_block_moe.py` | in place in modelopt_patched.py, idempotent | Stacks on top of the MXFP8 patch a branch that builds Fp8MoEMethod for FP8_BLOCK_SCALES / FP8_PB_WO experts. | get_quant_method returned None for that algorithm: the MoE was silently left unquantized and died at load (~7 min); the gap also exists upstream (d4d703ca and main). Enables MTP: 24.5 → 52.1 tok/s, acceptance 72.8% (823/1131). | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 11 Mixture-of-Experts |

#### Model architecture

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `files/overlay/hyperconnection.diff` | overlay FP8_DENSE | hyperconnection.py now accepts quant_config (previously quant_config=None was hard-coded). | HyperConnections are 1.19 GiB of replicated BF16 read every step (13%), a target for FP8-dense. | 15 Hyper-connections, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `files/overlay/model.diff` | overlay FP8_DENSE | model.py passes quant_config to the HyperConnections and to the lm_head. | Without propagating quant_config, these modules would stay BF16. | 30 vLLM architecture |
| `fix do YaRN no-op (--hf-overrides aninhado)` | single payload under text_config (ple_embedding_dtype + rope_parameters) | Replaces the root-level {rope_parameters:...} override with a single JSON nested under text_config. | ModelConfig._apply_dict_overrides only recurses into keys that are nested configs; for qwen4_exp the parent exposes rope_parameters as a plain dict, so the setattr never reached text_config and every earlier 1M run used unscaled RoPE. Result: 1M not validated, YARN_ENABLE defaults to false and MAX_MODEL_LEN is 262144. | 08 Position & RoPE, 24 Checkpoints |
| `guard YaRN ≤ nativo` | forces YARN_ENABLE=false if MAX_MODEL_LEN ≤ 262144 and prints a NOTE | Automatically disables YaRN when the requested context does not exceed the native one. | YaRN below 262144 only costs accuracy without extending anything. | 08 Position & RoPE |

#### Operations / Docker

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `.last_head_launch.sh` | permission 0600 | Stores the head's rendered launch command for inspection. | Allows auditing exactly what was launched; 0600 because it may contain --api-key. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `check-weights.sh` | [--verify \| --dry-run \| -n \| --manifest FILE] | Checks presence and size on the 2 nodes; --verify compares SHA-256 (LFS) and git blob SHA-1 against the HF API manifest (/api/models/{repo}/tree/{rev}?recursive=true); --manifest works offline. | A corrupted shard keeps a similar size and the engine only dies at ~33% of the load (issue #30); ~1 min for 135 GB at 2.4 GiB/s. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `docker save \| ssh docker load` | docker save vllm/vllm-openai:v0.30.0 \| ssh &lt;worker&gt; docker load | Transfers the Docker image from the head to a worker without internet access. | The image must exist on both nodes, and the worker may have no external route. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `download.sh` | [--fp8 \| org/repo] | Downloads the checkpoint to the head (snapshot_download(max_workers=4) for the ablit; otherwise uvx hf download / huggingface-cli / hf), resumable, validating completeness. | An interrupted download looked finished; validation avoids propagating a hole via rsync. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `files/*.orig` | delete after updating the image (includes ple_layer_patched.py, modelopt_patched.py.orig) | Copies of the original files extracted from the image, reused across launches. | Deleting them forces re-extraction from the new image; otherwise the patch would be applied on top of the old original. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `files/nfs-server/Dockerfile` | Alpine + nfs-utils (+ entrypoint.sh) | NFS server image used by nfs-share.sh. | Avoids installing and configuring NFS on the DGX Spark host. | 27 Unified memory, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `files/nfs-share.sh` | container --privileged --network host --restart unless-stopped | Brings up the NFSv4 server that exports $HF_CACHE_DIR read-only; reuses a live NFSv4 server and, if it is a foreign one, mounts via the absolute path device=:$HF_CACHE_DIR. | A foreign server's v4 pseudoroot is the host's / — a bug fixed on 2026-09-10. | 27 Unified memory, 24 Checkpoints |
| `files/overlay/apply_patches.py` | missing from the repo (.gitignore:50 ignores files/overlay/*.py) | Generator that start.sh runs to produce files/overlay/modelopt.py when it does not exist. | Only the .diff files are versioned: FP8_DENSE=true probably fails on a clean clone; you need a docker cp of the original + patch -p0. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `files/overlay/apply_patches.py (ausente)` | called by start.sh but not versioned | Script that should generate the FP8-dense overlay .py files from the .diff files. | ⚠ It is not in the repository (the .gitignore pattern files/overlay/*.py ignores it); only the .diff files exist, so FP8_DENSE=true does not work as is. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `files/resolve_snapshot.py` | exit 0/1/2 | Requires every shard in the weight_map to exist; prefers the complete refs/main, otherwise the newest complete snapshot. | An interrupted download looked finished, and rsync would copy the hole to the worker. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `fp8dense/build.sh` | creates models--MiaAI-Lab--Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-NVFP4-FP8dense | Resolves the source snapshot with resolve_snapshot.py, creates the destination repo with a refs/main that has no trailing newline, runs the converter and the verifier in the image in CPU-only mode, and runs chown. | Reproducible build of the hybrid checkpoint, with clear partial-download messages. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `guardas do start-tp1.sh` | ComfyUI, comfy-h3.service on 8888, insufficient KV, cap &gt; MemTotal − OS_RESERVE, MemAvailable &lt; cap + 4 | Checks that refuse to launch under dangerous memory or port conditions. | The host has already hard-crashed 3×; on UMA it is better not to start than to freeze the node. | 27 Unified memory, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `journalctl -k \| grep NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY` | continuous monitoring | Searches the kernel log for NVIDIA driver allocation errors. | On UMA it is the early sign of memory pressure before a hard reset; sweep.py records this count. | 27 Unified memory, 45 Benchmarking |
| `nfs-server/{Dockerfile,entrypoint.sh}` | privileged Alpine container | Container with the kernel nfsd exporting /export read-only as the NFSv4 pseudoroot (fsid=0). | It is the server used by nfs-share.sh, configured through NFS_OPTS. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `nfs-share.sh` | NFS_SHARE=true | Brings up the NFS server on the head exporting the HF cache and creates the NFS 4.2 Docker volume on the worker; detects a 'foreign' NFSv4 server and mounts via the absolute path. | Avoids copying ~126 GiB to the worker; the cold start streams the weights over the CX7 (lazy safetensors). | 27 Unified memory, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `normalização de refs/main` | rewrites only if the bytes differ | Removes the trailing newline from refs/main in the HF cache, comparing byte counts (since $(cat) would hide the newline). | hf_hub 1.28 reads refs/main without .strip(): echo $SHA produces a nonexistent snapshots/sha\n and vLLM dies with repo-not-found (issue #36). Write it with printf, not echo. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `parse_diff / find / apply (mini-aplicador de hunks + os.replace)` | idempotent, atomic .tmp write + os.replace | Embeds the upstream diff, applies unified hunks, detects 'already applied' and writes atomically. | The test confirms that it applies, is idempotent, fails without writing if the context changed, and passes straight through if already applied. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `patch_*.py (patch por âncoras + bind-mount :ro)` | the pattern used by every patch in the recipe | Extracts the original file from the image to files/{name}.orig, applies the patch by anchors (each anchor exactly once, validated with ast.parse) and mounts the output :ro on top of the vLLM package on both nodes. | The image is never rebuilt; the method is reproducible and auditable (the diff is the script) and fails loudly if the image changes. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `resolve_snapshot.py` | exit 0 complete / 1 incomplete / 2 nonexistent | Considers complete only a snapshot in which every shard in model.safetensors.index.json exists; prefers a complete refs/main, otherwise the newest complete one. | An interrupted download looked finished; a truncated shard passes a size check and only blows up at 33% of the load. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `ssh bash -s (script do worker por stdin)` | mktemp 0600; no scp to a fixed path | The worker script is sent over stdin via ssh "bash -s" &lt; script. | Leaves no file containing the command (which may include secrets) on the worker. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `stop.sh` | [--nfs \| --all] | docker rm -f vllm-fn on the worker and then on the head; --nfs takes down the share (15 s timeout) and removes the volume. | Required before switching checkpoint/lane (they all use the vllm-fn name and the same port); rpcbind in D-state may ignore SIGKILL. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `test_draft_vocab.py` | VOCAB 100, TP 2, 23 unbalanced ids | Runs the real patched functions with a fake vocab-parallel lm_head and a stubbed collective; compares against the full-vocabulary argmax restricted to the set and tests the empty rank. | Must be run in the image after any change to the patch. | 43 Qwen recipe, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `test_quant_config_resolution.py` | 22 ForConditionalGeneration prefixes + 8 CausalLM/MTP | With the overlay's modelopt.py mounted, checks the method resolved per prefix (in_proj_qkvz → FP8, in_proj_ba → EXCLUDED, mlp.experts → NVFP4, layers.1.ple.key_proj → EXCLUDED, lm_head → FP8, mtp.* → EXCLUDED). | 30/30 correct resolutions (fable §8.1). | 24 Checkpoints, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `verify-weights.py` | --repo --path --revision --manifest --save-manifest --fetch-only --dry-run --workers 8 --quiet | 8-thread hash verifier: SHA-256 of lfs.oid for LFS and git blob SHA-1 (blob size\0 + content) for git files; exit 0 ok / 1 weight problem / 2 tool failure. | The manifest is saved with repo/revision (it refuses a manifest from another repo); EXTRA files are listed without failing; allows --save-manifest where there is a route and --manifest on the nodes. | 24 Checkpoints |

#### Prefix caching

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `files/patch_block_drop.py` | backport vllm#53388 → files/block_drop/{config/speculative.py, v1/core/kv_cache_utils.py, v1/core/sched/scheduler.py} | Adds disable_eagle_block_drop to SpeculativeConfig and use_eagle_block_drop(), used by the scheduler and kv_cache_utils; skipped if the image already has the option. The 3 KV connectors are patched but not mounted; the sliding window fix is left out (QSA does not support it). | With EAGLE-like proposers, the prefix cache dropped the last matched block and recomputed it, making the 2nd turn miss the cache (#62): turn 1 TTFT 3.25 → 0.63 s, turn 2 1.12 → 0.59 s; decode/prefill/NLL unchanged. Log: EAGLE trailing prefix-cache block dropping is disabled. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 33 Prefix caching |
| `patch_block_drop.py` | MTP_DISABLE_BLOCK_DROP=1; generates 6 files, mounts 3 | Backport of vllm#53388: adds SpeculativeConfig.disable_eagle_block_drop and use_eagle_block_drop(). If the image already has the option, it removes old outputs and mounts nothing. | The KV connectors do not apply without a KV connector and would collide by basename; the 7th file (sliding window) was omitted because QSA does not support SWA. TTFT turn 1 3.25 → 0.63 s; turn 2 1.12 → 0.59 s; post-tool 2.55 → 1.98 s. | 33 Prefix caching, 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `use_eagle_block_drop()` | = use_eagle() and not disable_eagle_block_drop | kv_cache_utils, the scheduler (last_cache_position -= block_size only if active) and three connectors (mooncake, offloading, simple CPU offload) switch to the new check. | 'The drafter still runs. The change can move acceptance only'. | 33 Prefix caching, 32 Scheduler & batching |

| `_dequantize_embeddings` | NVFP4 branches (on-device and offload) and FP8 | Dequantizes the fetched PLE rows: NVFP4 with two scale levels, or FP8 as emb.to(bf16) * weight_scale (one global scale per tensor). | Only the rows used in the step become BF16. | 20 Quantization fundamentals, 16 N-gram & MTP |

| `_get_ple_embedding_quant_method` | order: dtype NVFP4 → FP8 → Fp8Config → ModelOptNvFp4Config → None (BF16) | Decides the PLE method: ple_embedding_dtype NVFP4 → NVFP4; FP8 → FP8; serialized Fp8Config → FP8; ModelOptNvFp4Config with the PLE not excluded → NVFP4 (excluded but dtype FP8 → FP8); otherwise BF16. | Resolves the PLE correctly in mixed checkpoints, with the constructor passing getattr(config, 'ple_embedding_dtype', None). | 24 Checkpoints, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `add_aliases (mtp.layers.{48+i})` | also mirrored into config_groups[*].targets | Adds mtp.layers.{48+i}.* for each mtp.layers.{i}.* in quantized_layers. | vLLM builds the MTP at the absolute index that continues the 48-layer stack, and nvidia only registers mtp.layers.0.*. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `compute_quant_stats.py` | output: fp8_dense_quant_stats.json | Recomputes the relative RMSE of all 591 FP8 tensors in FP8-dense. | min/median/max 0.0240 / 0.0262 / 0.0268; the worst are the small block_inject_weight gates. | 25 Measuring quality, 20 Quantization fundamentals |

Keeps embeddings, router gates, the QSA indexer, in_proj_a/b, conv1d, norms | 24 Checkpoints, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
