| `files/overlay/modelopt.diff` | FP8_PER_CHANNEL_PER_TOKEN → ModelOptFp8PcPtLinearMethod; FP8_PB_WO → ModelOptFp8PbWoLinearMethod | Makes modelopt.py dispatch the new quant_algo values and accept extra prefixes (model. → model.language_model., lm_head). | Without these methods the FP8-dense checkpoint does not load; +≈3.5 GiB/GPU of KV is expected. | 30 vLLM architecture, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
| `files/patch_checkpoint_config.py` | → files/config_patched.json and files/hf_quant_config_patched.json mounted over the snapshot | Adds .0. → .48. aliases in quantized_layers and config_groups.targets and rewrites FP8_PB_WO (group_size 128) → FP8_BLOCK_SCALES in the mtp.*.mlp.experts entries. | vLLM creates the MTP at mtp.layers.48 (num_hidden_layers + i) and matches metadata by exact string, but nvidia only stores mtp.layers.0.*; config.json and hf_quant_config.json can disagree (rev fc694b54), and the MoE dispatch reads config.json (issue #38). The HF cache is never modified. | 24 Checkpoints, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `files/patch_modelopt_fp8_block_moe.py` | in place over modelopt_patched.py; Fp8Config(weight_block_size=[g,g]) with g taken from group_size | Adds an FP8_BLOCK_SCALES branch to ModelOptMixedPrecisionConfig.get_quant_method, building Fp8Config(is_checkpoint_fp8_serialized=True, activation_scheme=dynamic) and routing to Fp8MoEMethod; refuses to guess the block. | Without it, the MTP's 128×128 FP8 experts silently become an unquantized MoE that dies ~7 min later (no parameter w2_weight_scale_inv); the gap also exists upstream. With the fix: 52.1 tok/s, 72.8% acceptance with an 89/74.5/60% curve (near-random acceptance would indicate a wrong block). | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `files/patch_modelopt_mxfp8.py` | → files/modelopt_patched.py at .../quantization/modelopt.py; always on the day-0 lane | ModelOptMxFp8LinearMethod.create_weights checks the post-TP-split (N,K) and, outside the mm_mxfp8 envelope, swaps in EmulationMxfp8LinearKernel; all of visual.* goes to emulation by prefix; logs 1 WARNING per shape. | mm_mxfp8 requires N ≥ 128, N%32==0, K ≥ 128, K%32==0 on sm_121: in_proj_a/b [48, 2560] (×72) raised a fatal AssertionError in the profiling run (~7 min) and visual linear_fc1 [4304,1152] (×27) raised Problem size is not supported. Emulating only vision avoids the OOM of a global dequant; ≈17 MB extra. | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 28 Kernels |
| `files/patch_ple_layer.py` | → files/ple_layer_patched.py mounted at .../qwen3_8_flash_next/nvidia/ple_layer.py; skipped with SKIP_PLE_PATCH | Ports the dispatch from vLLM PR #53899: a new Qwen3_8FlashNextPLENVFp4EmbeddingMethod (uint8 dim/2 rows + FP8 scales per block of 16 + FP32 global scale, dequant LUT), _get_ple_embedding_quant_method deciding by ple_embedding_dtype, and the FP8 method handling FP8 shards + one gl | The stock image only picks the FP8 PLE if the whole checkpoint is Fp8Config; with an NVFP4 body it builds a BF16 embedding (~102 GB) and crashes. The offload fixes eliminate the fluent gibberish. | 16 N-gram & MTP, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
| `load_weights (PLE, reescrito)` | slices shards per TP rank | Skips hashstats_* and token_lookup, copies persistent buffers (layer_multipliers, ngram_heads_offsets, ngram_heads_vocab_sizes) while validating their shape and, for NVFP4, collects codes and scales per shard, slicing them to the TP rank's vocabulary range. | Loads the PLE sharded by row range across the ranks (≈24 GiB FP8 per GPU) and keeps the global scale weight_scale_2. | 24 Checkpoints, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `make_fp8_dense_checkpoint.py` | FP8_DENSE=true (experimental) | Hard-links (from the blob) the non-BF16 shards, rewrites only the model-bf16-* shards in streaming fashion (QUANT_PATTERNS → F8_E4M3 + F32 weight_scale [out]) with its own safetensors writer (header first, then seek) and writes config.json, hf_quant_config.json and the index. | The BF16 dense layers are ~6.9 of the ~9.2 GiB read per step. ~2 min build with ~1 GB of RAM: 202 shards hard-linked, 4 rewritten (11.8 GB), 123 GiB index. Expected ~1.5× at batch 1 (fable: 85-90 tok/s), never measured on GPU. | 24 Checkpoints, 20 Quantization fundamentals |
| `ModelOptMxFp8EmulationLinearMethod` | EmulationMxfp8LinearKernel when the native kernel does not accept the shape | Dequantizes MXFP8 → BF16 once at load time and runs the layer as a BF16 linear; logs once per shape. | Trades a few MB of memory for kernel compatibility, decided using the post-TP-split dimensions the kernel will see. | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `normalize_mtp_fp8_pb_wo` | FP8_PB_WO (group_size 128) → FP8_BLOCK_SCALES | Rewrites the MTP experts' label in the patched config. | Normalizes ModelOpt's new label (nvidia fc694b54, Keys, ABLIT) to an algorithm the dispatch knows. | 24 Checkpoints, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
| `overlay/hyperconnection.diff` | FP8_DENSE=true (2026-09-02) | GatedResidual.__init__ gains quant_config and passes it to the three linears (input_mix_weight_down_block_inject with disable_tp=True, input_mix_weight_down, input_mix_weight_up) instead of a hard-coded None. | Allows quantizing the replicated HC linears (1.19 GiB bf16, 13% of the step); for unquantized checkpoints it becomes UnquantizedLinearMethod. | 15 Hyper-connections, 24 Checkpoints |
| `overlay/model.diff` | FP8_DENSE=true | Passes quant_config to each layer's two GatedResidual modules, to the final hyper_connection_mixer and to the ParallelLMHead. | Without it, these modules ignore the quantization_config and stay in BF16. | 15 Hyper-connections, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `overlay/modelopt.diff` | FP8_DENSE=true | In ModelOptMixedPrecisionConfig's from_config, creates fp8_pcpt_config (FP8_PER_CHANNEL_PER_TOKEN) and fp8_pbwo_config (FP8_PB_WO), adds prefix candidates (model. → model.language_model., lm_head) and dispatches to ModelOptFp8PcPtLinearMethod / ModelOptFp8PbWoLinearMethod. | Expected kernel: CutlassFP8ScaledMMLinearKernel (sm_120 cubins that the GB10, cc 12.1, runs). ⚠ Conflicts on the same path with modelopt_patched.py (start.sh:580 and :866). | 30 vLLM architecture, 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling |
| `overlay/mtp.diff` | FP8_DENSE=true | Passes quant_config to the MTP drafter's mixer and lm_head. | ⚠ Mounts nvidia/mtp.py, the same file as the draft vocab patch (start.sh refuses to use both together). | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 24 Checkpoints |
| `patch_checkpoint_config.py` | whenever there is a snapshot; generates config_patched.json and hf_quant_config_patched.json | Adds the MTP layer aliases and normalizes FP8_PB_WO; the JSON files are mounted over the snapshot inside the container, without touching the HF cache. | Without it: 'Layer mtp.layers.48.mlp.experts has no parameter w2_weight_scale_inv' (the MTP MoE is built unquantized). | 24 Checkpoints, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `patch_modelopt_mxfp8.py` | always (except on the v0.30 lane); output: modelopt_patched.py | MXFP8 → BF16 emulation fallback in modelopt.py: swaps the kernel per layer when the post-TP shape is unsupported and always sends visual.* to emulation. | Avoids the crash on the first forward of the profile run (~7 min); costs ~17 MB extra across the 72 in_proj_a/b. ⚠ (inferred) Probably inert on the nvidia checkpoint; needed for checkpoints with MXFP8 layers. | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 43 Qwen recipe |
| `patch_ple_layer.py → ple_layer_patched.py` | always (except with SKIP_PLE_PATCH=true); port of vLLM PR #53899 | Mixed quantization dispatch for the PLE table and fixes to the offload path, mounted at vllm/models/qwen3_8_flash_next/nvidia/ple_layer.py. | The image only chose FP8 for the PLE when the whole checkpoint was Fp8Config; in MIXED_PRECISION checkpoints the PLE became a ≈102 GB BF16 table and the load crashed. Without the patch, the checkpoint does not load. | 16 N-gram & MTP, 24 Checkpoints |
| `Qwen3_8FlashNextPLENVFp4EmbeddingMethod` | weight uint8 [rows, dim/2] + weight_scale e4m3 [rows, dim/16] + weight_scale_2 fp32 | New NVFP4 method for the PLE: the lookup returns 80 + 10 = 90 B per head, and _dequant_nvfp4_codes splits the nibbles, uses the E2M1 LUT (0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6) with a sign bit and multiplies by block scale × global scale. | The table stays packed in memory and only the 16 rows looked up per token are dequantized (26.8 GiB instead of 95.4 GiB in BF16). | 21 FP8, FP4 & microscaling, 16 N-gram & MTP |
| `verify_fp8_dense_checkpoint.py` | relative error ≤ 0.06 | Checks that all source tensors exist, that each FP8 tensor has an F32 [rows] scale, that untouched shards are the same inode and that dequant samples have acceptable error and cosine. | Measured cosine ≥ 0.9996. | 25 Measuring quality, 24 Checkpoints |

#### Sampling / generation

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `files/patch_determinism.py` | in-place in qsa_ops_patched.py + files/determinism/flashinfer_cutlass_moe.py; opt-in | After topk_op, sorts the indices with -1 → INT32_MAX (sentinels at the tail) if VLLM_QSA_DET_TOPK=1, and sets use_fused_finalize = VLLM_MOE_DET_FINALIZE != 1. | The top-k order and the fused finalize varied between identical runs in TP2+EP: spread 0.25/6.07 nats → 0/0, outputs 5/5 → 1/5 distinct, cost −4%. | 09 Generation & sampling, 25 Measuring quality |

#### Speculative decoding

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_attach_draft_vocab` | filters ids in the rank's [org_vocab_start_index, org_vocab_end_index) range | After load_weights, reads VLLM_MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB, copies only the rank's rows into _draft_lm_head_weight and stores the _draft_id_to_target_id map; refuses to engage if the logit scale is ≤ 0. | Log: '47149 of 248320 tokens (19.0%), 45734 on this rank of 2; draft lm_head shard 0.59 → 0.22 GiB per draft step'. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 38 Parallelism |
| `bench/gen_draft_corpus.py` | --per-prompt 2, --max-tokens 1200, --temperature 0.8, top_p 0.95, --concurrency 4 · corpus from the model's own output | Generates a .jsonl corpus from the model's own output. · Generates the corpus used by build_draft_vocab.py from the model's own output on the target traffic type. | The drafter needs to predict the model's output distribution, not that of generic text. · The draft vocabulary should reflect what the model generates, not a generic corpus. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 45 Benchmarking |
| `build_draft_vocab.py` | prints coverage at 8k/16k/32k/64k/128k | Counts id frequencies in a corpus (ideally the model's own output), keeps special and added tokens, ranks them, and optionally fills and balances them across TP ranges. | Choose the size by coverage of real traffic; heavily padded vocabularies tank the acceptance rate. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `draft_vocab_en_code_47k.txt` | 47,149 sorted, unique ids, from 0 to 248,076 | List of ids (one per line) of the draft vocabulary, vendored unchanged from PR #39 of the single-Spark repo and tuned on 30 MiB of code+docs. | 100% corpus coverage, 99.58% held-out; dense in low ids (≈97% on rank 0); the last 33 ids (248044-248076) are special tokens. It may draft worse in other languages (especially Chinese). Since 288369d (2026-09-29) there are 65k versions that extend this file per language (draft_vocab_{lang}_en_code_65k.txt). | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `draft_vocab_{lang}_en_code_65k.txt` | 7 files (es, zh, ja, de, pt, fr, ru) of 65,536 ids; opt-in through MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB; the default is still 47k | Language-extended draft vocabularies: the 47,149-id file whole as a floor, the 400 byte-fallback ids pinned, and 18,387 ids added by frequency over 668 MiB of that language's Wikipedia (files/build_draft_vocab_extend.py). Switch with MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB=files/draft_vocab_{lang}_en_co | Held-out coverage (68 MiB of disjoint Wikipedia), 47k → 65k: es 63.8 → 99.3%, zh 34.7 → 96.7%, ja 30.0 → 99.7%, de 60.2 → 99.5%, pt 65.4 → 99.2%, fr 69.4 → 99.5%, ru 31.5 → 99.7%. Acceptance per draft (TP=2, one boot per arm, temperature 0, thinking off; German in ABBA): ru 0.39 → 1.55, ja 0.45 → 1.34, zh 0.45 → 1.14, de 0.81 → 1.40, es 1 | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 43 Qwen recipe |
| `files/build_draft_vocab.py` | corpus...[:N], --size 32768, --report-only, --balance-shards (=TP), --fill-to-size, --model (DRAFT_VOCAB_MODEL) | Builds the reduced-vocabulary id file and reports coverage at 8k/16k/32k/65k/131k, always keeping special/added tokens. | Tune on coverage, not size: the old corpus had only 8,305 distinct ids and saturated at 16,384; lowest-id fill put 65,392 of 65,536 on rank 0, and --balance-shards 2 dropped the acceptance to 47.8%. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 38 Parallelism |
| `files/build_draft_vocab_extend.py` | --base (47k) --corpus … --size 65536 --byte-fallback-max 400 --tp-report 2 --out | Extends a shipped draft vocabulary with a second language; ported from the single-Spark recipe's method, with a report of the id spread per TP shard. Three rules: the 47k goes in whole as a floor; the byte-fallback range is always pinned; ids are only added by frequency over real | The method exists because, in the single-Spark recipe, rebuilding from scratch with a dictionary as corpus dropped 90 byte-fallback ids and destroyed Spanish. Since the lm_head is vocab-parallel and a step waits for the slowest rank, the script shows how many ids land in each rank's range; corpus-ranked ids are never moved to balance. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `files/draft_vocab_en_code_47k.txt` | 47,149 ids (19.0% of 248,320); MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB default | List of frequent English and code ids used by the drafter. | Log: 47149 of 248320 tokens (19.0%), 45734 on this rank of 2; draft lm_head shard 0.59 -&gt; 0.22 GiB. Chinese may draft worse. For other languages: draft_vocab_{lang}_en_code_65k.txt (288369d). | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `files/overlay/mtp.diff` | FP8_DENSE overlay; conflicts with MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB | mtp.py passes quant_config to the drafter's HyperConnections and lm_head. | Both override nvidia/mtp.py, which is why FP8_DENSE and the reduced draft vocab are incompatible. | 30 vLLM architecture, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `files/patch_mtp_draft_vocab.py` | → files/mtp_patched.py (nvidia/mtp.py); test files/test_draft_vocab.py | _attach_draft_vocab (after load_weights) reads VLLM_MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB, keeps only the ids in the rank's [org_vocab_start_index, org_vocab_end_index) range and does index_select → _draft_lm_head_weight + _draft_id_to_target_id; get_top_tokens does a reduced matmul, a local argmax an | +9.6% on average (09-11), +8.4% (09-16), all of it in ms/step (code 61.2→54.0, prose 59.9→53.3 ms), tokens/step unchanged. 45,734 ids on rank 0 and 1,415 on rank 1: the step waits for the slowest rank. The logit scale and the tanh soft-cap are monotonic and can be skipped. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 38 Parallelism |
| `files/patch_mtp_draft_vocab_v030.py` | the only mandatory overlay of the v0.30 lane | Reapplies the draft vocab patch blocks on top of qwen4_exp's mtp.py. | Stock v0.30 is 5–15% slower because it drafts over 248k (58.3 ms/step at S=1). | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 43 Qwen recipe |
| `get_top_tokens` | all-gather of (value, global_id) | logits = hidden @ W_draftᵀ with a local argmax; a rank with no ids makes a losing −inf bid; with TP greater than 1 it all-gathers the (value, global id) pair and picks the winner. Skips the scale and soft-cap because they are monotonic. | Costs O(batch·2·tp) instead of O(batch·vocab). | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 38 Parallelism |
| `patch_mtp_draft_vocab.py` | MTP_DRAFT_VOCAB non-empty (default) | TP-aware reduced vocabulary (FR-Spec) for the MTP drafter's lm_head in nvidia/mtp.py; compute_logits keeps the full vocabulary. | Safe: the drafter is greedy and a token outside the subset merely becomes a rejected draft. The gain is entirely in step time (code S=1 61.2 → 54.0 ms; prose 59.9 → 53.3 ms): +8.4 to +9.6%. | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab |
| `patch_mtp_draft_vocab_v030.py` | vLLM 0.30 lane | Reuses the same draft vocab code blocks for vLLM 0.30's qwen4_exp/nvidia/mtp.py. | The stock v0.30 lane (full vocab) sits at 58.3 ms/step S=1 (5-15% slower); the port brings it back to parity with day-0 (±4%). | 36 MTP, EAGLE & reduced vocab, 43 Qwen recipe |

### Parameters (env vars, flags, configs, profiles)

#### API / serving

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--enable-prompt-tokens-details` | always (CHANGELOG 2026-09-25, #63) | Exposes usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens in responses. | It is only observability metadata: it neither enables nor retunes prefix caching; in streaming it requires stream_options.include_usage. | 33 Prefix caching, 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `--host` | 0.0.0.0 (head node only) | Bind address of the API server. | Exposes the API without authentication on all interfaces; with the abliterated checkpoint, responsibility for safeguards falls on the operator. | 34 API, tools & JSON, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `endpoints OpenAI` | /v1/chat/completions, /v1/completions, /v1/models, /tokenize, /detokenize, /metrics, /health, /version, /docs | HTTP surface exposed by the head node. | The launcher polls /health until it gets 200; longctx.py calibrates prompts with /tokenize. | 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `PORT` | 8888 (--port only on the head) | OpenAI API port on the head, with --network host. | Only rank 0 serves the API; on TP1, comfy-h3.service also listens on 8888 (HANDOFF §0) and the script refuses to launch. | 34 API, tools & JSON, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `SERVED_MODEL_NAME` | qwen3.8-flash-next (FP8: qwen3.8-flash-next-fp8) | Name exposed at /v1/models via --served-model-name. | It is the value clients send in the model field of their requests. | 34 API, tools & JSON |
| `stream_options.include_usage` | true | Makes the last streaming chunk carry the usage object. | It is the only way to see cached_tokens when streaming. | 34 API, tools & JSON, 33 Prefix caching |

#### Abliteration

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ABLIT` | 0 / 1 → drowzeys/keys-Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-NVFP4-dual-ablit-house-qsa-L3-47 (gated) · abliterated checkpoint (optional) | With 1, serves an abliterated checkpoint with the same layout as nvidia NVFP4, with a house residual-writer projection on 12 self_attn.o_proj tensors of QSA layers 3,7,11,…,47. · Derived checkpoint with a projection applied to the 12 QSA o_proj tensors to remove refusals. | GDN, experts, MTP, PLE, vision and the chat template stay intact; it only applies to this recipe (not with MXFP8 single-Spark, official FP8 or FP8_DENSE) and is ignored if FP8_DENSE=true or OVERRIDE_MODEL_ID is set. Shifts the safeguards to the operator. · Relevant to the patches because its config.json labels the MTP experts as FP8_PB_WO | 19 Abliteration, 43 Qwen recipe |

#### Attention / kernels

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `indexer_budget` | 2048 (512 blocks × 4) + up to 3 from the current block = up to 2051 | How many positions QSA selects before doing sparse GQA over the original K/V. | Per-token attention is capped at ≤2051 positions, whatever the context length. | 13 Sparse attention |
| `indexer_compress_ratio` | 4 | 4-token micro-blocks summarized into one compressed key (FP32 mean, normalized, with RoPE). | The indexer cache becomes [pages, 1600/4, 1, 128]; scoring blocks costs 4× less than scoring tokens. | 13 Sparse attention |
| `indexer_n_heads / indexer_head_dim / indexer_kv_heads` | 4 / 128 / 1 | Shape of the QSA's lightweight indexer: 4 Q heads of 128 dims and 1 shared K head. | The score is s = (1/√128) Σ_h ReLU(⟨q_h, k̄_b⟩) over compressed keys; the indexer cache has 1 head × 128. | 13 Sparse attention |
| `num_attention_heads / num_key_value_heads` | 24 / 2 (TP2: 12 Q and 1 KV per GPU) | Q and K/V heads of the full attention (QSA). | GQA 24:2 shrinks the KV; on TP2 each GPU gets 24 // tp = 12 Q and max(1, 2 // tp) = 1 KV. | 12 MLA & compact KV, 06 Attention |
| `output_gate_type` | sigmoid | Output-gated attention: each head's output is multiplied by the sigmoid of an extra projection. | Explains the qkv+gate projection (6656, 2560) on TP2 = 3072 q + 256 k + 256 v + 3072 gate (inferred decomposition). | 06 Attention, 18 The three models |

#### Benchmark / quality

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/metrics` | vllm:spec_decode_num_accepted_tokens_per_pos_total, spec_decode_num_drafts_total, spec_decode_num_draft_tokens_total, spec_decode_num_accepted_tokens_total, generation_tokens_total | The engine's cumulative Prometheus counters. | It mixes all clients: always measure deltas around the workload (tok/step = 1 + Δaccepted/Δdrafts). | 45 Benchmarking, 35 Speculation: fundamentals |
| `bench_qsa_kernels.py (--tp / --rows / --ctx / --out)` | --tp 2, --rows 1 4 8 32, --ctx 4096 32768 131072, --out qsa_profile.json | Micro-benchmark that sweeps block_n∈{16,32,64} × splits∈{1..64} × warps∈{2,4} and MQA tiles∈{1,2,4,8,16} × warps∈{1,2,4}, picking the config that wins most often per bucket. | Uses real shapes (12 q-heads × 1 kv-head × 256, page 1600, top-k 2048+3, indexer cache [pages, 400, 1, 128]); generates the JSON for QSA_PROFILE. | 28 Kernels, 45 Benchmarking |
| `ignore_eos` | true in the decode benches | Ignores the end-of-sequence token so the requested number of tokens is always generated. | A fixed-size decode window makes tok/s comparable across arms. | 45 Benchmarking, 09 Generation & sampling |
| `min_tokens` | min_tokens = max_tokens | Forces the model to generate at least N tokens. | With an equal max_tokens, every request has exactly the same output length. | 45 Benchmarking, 09 Generation & sampling |
| `VLLM_TORCH_PROFILER_DIR` | present, not used | Directory where vLLM writes torch profiler traces. | A tool for finding out which kernels dominate the step. | 45 Benchmarking, 28 Kernels |

#### Distributed / network

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--distributed-executor-backend` | mp (always; not Ray) | vLLM's native multiprocess executor, one process per node. · Uses vLLM's multiprocess executor. | It is the only one that spawns the PLE offload worker (HANDOFF §4) and auto-enables async scheduling with MTP (fable §1). · It is the executor that creates the PLE offload process (required by the offload). | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `--enable-expert-parallel` | on | Expert parallel: each GPU holds half of the experts (256). | EP is mandatory for NVFP4 in the recipe; with DP=1 the tokens are replicated and EP only changes which weights each GPU holds. | 38 Parallelism, 11 Mixture-of-Experts |
| `--headless` | worker only | Runs the worker without an API server. | Only rank 0 has an API server; the worker only runs the model. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `--master-addr` | $HEAD_IP · the head's IP | Rendezvous address of the distributed group. · torch.distributed rendezvous address on the head. | Both ranks need to reach this IP over the CX7 link. · The two nodes need to find each other to form the NCCL group over RoCE. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `--nnodes` | 2 (fixed) | Number of nodes in vLLM's native distributed group, without Ray. · Number of nodes in the multi-node deployment. | Each node runs one process; the world size is 2 GPUs. · TP=2 crosses the RoCE/ConnectX-7 network, with no NVLink (PyNCCL with all-reduce only). | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `--node-rank` | 0 (head) / 1 (worker) | Position of each node in the distributed group. | The worker (rank 1) comes up first and the head waits 15 s before coming up; otherwise the rendezvous fails. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `--tensor-parallel-size` | 2 (1 GPU per Spark) | Splits the matrices across the 2 GPUs, one per node. | ≈106 small collectives per step (36 GDN + 12 attention + 48 MoE + 1 logits gather + 9 from the drafts), 5-20 KB each, 2-3 ms per step over the network. | 38 Parallelism, 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `GLOO_SOCKET_IFNAME` | =$IFACE (head) / =$WORKER_IFACE (worker) | Interface Gloo uses for CPU collectives and bootstrap. | If Gloo picks the wrong NIC, init hangs or becomes slow. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `HEAD_IP` | 10.0.0.1 (required) | The head's (rank 0) inter-node IP, used in --master-addr and in the head's VLLM_HOST_IP. | It is the distributed group's rendezvous point; in the kit 10.0.0.x is an alias and NFS must not use 10.0.0.1 (README:172). | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 41 Recipe engineering |
| `IB_GID_INDEX` | 3 (alternative 5) | Index of the RoCE GID (v2/IPv4) used by NCCL. | If 3 does not come up, try 5 (README:980); a wrong GID shows up as a silent hang during init. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `IB_HCA` | =rocep1s0f0 (live =rocep1s0f1); a leading = means an exact match of one device | RDMA HCA that NCCL uses on the head, passed as NCCL_IB_HCA. | Never point it at a port cabled to another cluster: NCCL hangs during init with no useful error (README:978). | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `IFACE` | enp1s0f0np0 (live enp1s0f1np1) | Ethernet interface of the ConnectX link on the head, used in GLOO_/NCCL_/TP_SOCKET_IFNAME. | It must be the interface of the cable between the Sparks (ip -o -4 addr); if the bootstrap lands on the wrong NIC you get a hang or slow traffic. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `MASTER_PORT` | 50000 (--master-port) | TCP port for coordinating the distributed group on the head. | Required for the torch.distributed rendezvous between the two nodes. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_ALGO` | Ring (suggested, not adopted) | Pins NCCL's collective algorithm. | With 2 nodes the ring is the natural path; pinning it avoids bad choices by the automatic tuning. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_CROSS_NIC` | 1 with NCCL_IB_HCA==rocep1s0f1,roceP2p1s0f1 (second CX7 link; suggested) | Allows NCCL to use different NICs on each side and, together with a second HCA, two CX7 links. | Doubles cross-node bandwidth (~2× prefill communication) without changing decode latency. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_CUMEM_ENABLE` | 0 (suggested, not adopted) | Disables the use of cuMem (VMM) for NCCL buffers. | Part of the fabric knob bundle suggested by fable §3.5 (combined estimate +3–8% decode): ~106 small cross-node collectives (5–20 KB) per step, 20–30 µs each, latency-bound; individual effect not measured. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 27 Unified memory |
| `NCCL_DEBUG` | WARN (fixed); INFO in fabric tests | Verbosity of the NCCL logs. | WARN keeps logs clean in production; to validate the fabric use INFO (and NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS) and look for GDRDMA/dmabuf. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_DEBUG_SUBSYS` | INIT,NET (suggested, not adopted) | Filters the subsystems NCCL logs. | Recommended after fabric changes to confirm the chosen transport, HCA and GID. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_DMABUF_ENABLE` | 1 (suggested, not adopted) | Enables GPU memory registration via dmabuf for GPUDirect RDMA. | nvidia_peermem is not loaded; on the GB10 with UMA it is unclear whether NCCL goes through host buffers — check for GDRDMA/dmabuf in the log. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 27 Unified memory |
| `NCCL_IB_AUTO_DETECT` | 0 (fixed) | Disables NCCL's HCA auto-detection. | Forces the use of the exact configured HCA, preventing NCCL from picking a port connected to another cluster. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_IB_DISABLE` | 0 (fixed) | Enables NCCL's IB/RoCE transport. | Without it NCCL would fall back to a TCP socket, much slower and with more latency per collective. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_IB_QPS_PER_CONNECTION` | 2..4 (suggested, not adopted) | Number of RDMA queue pairs per connection. | Part of the fabric knob bundle suggested by fable §3.5 (combined estimate +3–8% decode): ~106 small cross-node collectives (5–20 KB) per step, 20–30 µs each, latency-bound; individual effect not measured. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_IB_ROCE_VERSION_NUM` | 2 (suggested, not adopted) | Forces the RoCE version used by NCCL. | Part of the fabric knob bundle estimated at +3–8% decode; decode does ~106 small latency-dominated collectives per step. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_IB_SPLIT_DATA_ON_QPS` | 0 (suggested, not adopted) | Controls whether NCCL splits each message across QPs. | Part of the fabric knob bundle suggested by fable §3.5 (combined estimate +3–8% decode): ~106 small cross-node collectives (5–20 KB) per step, 20–30 µs each, latency-bound; individual effect not measured. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_IGNORE_CPU_AFFINITY` | 1 (suggested, not adopted) | Makes NCCL ignore the CPU affinity inherited from the process. | Part of the fabric knob bundle suggested by fable §3.5 (combined estimate +3–8% decode): ~106 small cross-node collectives (5–20 KB) per step, 20–30 µs each, latency-bound; individual effect not measured. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_NET` | IB (suggested, not adopted) | Forces NCCL to use the IB (verbs) transport instead of choosing automatically. | Avoids a silent fallback to sockets; suggested together with the other fabric knobs. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_NET_GDR_LEVEL` | SYS (suggested, not adopted) | Topology level up to which NCCL uses GPUDirect RDMA. | SYS allows GDR at any distance, part of the attempt to take host buffers out of the path. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_NET_PLUGIN` | none (suggested, not adopted) | Disables NCCL's external network plugins. | Part of the fabric knob bundle suggested by fable §3.5 (combined estimate +3–8% decode): ~106 small cross-node collectives (5–20 KB) per step, 20–30 µs each, latency-bound; individual effect not measured. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_NVLS_ENABLE` | 0 (suggested, not adopted) | Disables NVLink SHARP. | Part of the knob bundle suggested by fable §3.5; the Sparks are connected only via CX7 (no NVLink), and MNNVL does not exist on sm_121. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_PROTO` | LL\|LL128 (suggested, not adopted) | Restricts NCCL's protocols to the low-latency ones. | Decode messages are 5–20 KB and cost 20–30 µs each; LL protocols reduce the latency of small messages. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME` | =$IFACE / =$WORKER_IFACE | Interface NCCL uses for the TCP bootstrap (address exchange before RDMA). | Must be the interface of the CX7 link between the Sparks. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TENSOR_PARALLEL_SIZE` | 2 (1 GPU/node × 2 nodes) | Tensor parallel spanning both nodes (--tensor-parallel-size). | ~106 collectives per decode step: 36 GDN out_proj AR + 12 o_proj AR + 48 MoE AR (20 KB) + 1 logits all-gather (4 × 124160 × fp32 ≈ 2 MB) + 9 from the drafts; 2–3 ms/step (6–8%), latency-bound, does not go down with batch size. | 38 Parallelism, 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TORCH_NCCL_DUMP_ON_TIMEOUT` | 1 (not used) | Makes PyTorch dump the state of the NCCL collectives on a timeout. | Helps diagnose NCCL hangs between the nodes. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TORCH_NCCL_ENABLE_MONITORING` | 1 (not used) | Turns on PyTorch's NCCL collective watchdog. | Detects stuck collectives instead of hanging silently. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `TP_SOCKET_IFNAME` | =$IFACE / =$WORKER_IFACE | Interface for the TensorPipe/torch.distributed control sockets. | Keeps all control traffic on the same NIC as the link between the nodes. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `VLLM_HOST_IP` | $HEAD_IP / $WORKER_IP | IP that each node advertises to vLLM's control plane (ZMQ). | The per-step scheduler→worker broadcast already goes over the CX7 (~0.15–0.4 ms RTT, fable §3.5). | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 30 vLLM architecture |
| `WORKER_IB_HCA` | not set → IB_HCA (live =rocep1s0f0) | RDMA HCA used by NCCL on the worker. | Needed when the nodes are cross-wired and each side uses a different port. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `WORKER_IFACE` | not set → IFACE (live enp1s0f0np0) | Network interface on the worker side. | The nodes may be cross-wired (head f1, worker f0; README:982), so the worker's interface differs from the head's. | 39 NCCL & RDMA |
| `WORKER_IP` | 10.0.0.2 (required) | IP of the worker (rank 1), used in the worker's VLLM_HOST_IP and in ssh/scp/rsync. | Without it the head cannot distribute the weights or launch the remote container. | 39 NCCL & RDMA, 41 Recipe engineering |

#### Inference engine

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--compilation-config` | mode 0 (no torch.compile/Inductor); mode 3 suggested, not adopted · {'mode':0,'cudagraph_mode':'FULL_DECODE_ONLY'} | Compilation configuration: mode 0 turns off torch.compile/Inductor. · No torch.compile (mode 0), with CUDA graphs captured only for pure-decode batches. | Stability and startup time; fable §3.6 suggests testing mode 3 + FULL_AND_PIECEWISE (0–8%, +2–5 min of cold start) — not adopted. · Removes CPU overhead from decode; batches are rounded up to captured sizes and the padding uses NULL_BLOCK_ID (handled in the PLE). Graphs take ~7 s at cold start. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `--load-format` | safetensors (always) | Forces the safetensors loader for the checkpoint shards. | Pairs with the lazy strategy to read the weights via mmap on demand. | 24 Checkpoints |
| `--max-model-len` | 262144 (MAX_MODEL_LEN) | Maximum context per request. | YaRN only if you need more than 262k, always nested in text_config. With fp8 KV the concurrency at 262k is 13.93×. Re-measure after changing it. | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 08 Position & RoPE |
| `--performance-mode` | {balanced, interactivity, throughput} (not used) | Engine performance profile; interactivity captures CUDA graphs of every size 1…32. | Estimated 0–2% gain; not adopted. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup, 32 Scheduler & batching |
| `cudagraph_mode` | FULL_DECODE_ONLY (fixed); TP1 CUDAGRAPH_MODE=NONE for debugging; FULL_AND_PIECEWISE suggested | Full CUDA graphs only for decode batches, inside --compilation-config. | Captures 1–64 in 0.39 GiB (another measurement: 0.54 GiB) and eliminates launch overhead where it matters (decode). | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `CUDAGRAPH_MODE` | FULL_DECODE_ONLY / NONE for eager debugging | Configurable cudagraph_mode in the TP1 lane. | NONE allows debugging in eager mode without captured graphs. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |
| `MAX_MODEL_LEN` | 262144 (native); 1000000 with YaRN, 512000; TP1 65536 | Maximum context per request (--max-model-len); must be a positive integer. | Defines per-step structures even for short prompts: at 1M the QSA scorer allocates an fp32 buffer [rows, page_table_width×page_size] per layer (250K columns, 4 MB per verify pass per layer), persistent_topk over 250K columns and block tables with 625 entries; 262144 is recommended for agentic use (+2–5% expected). | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 13 Sparse attention |
| `V030_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH` | 0 (default) / 1 restores upstream · 0 (v0.30 lane default) → VLLM_USE_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH | Value passed to VLLM_USE_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH in the v0.30 lane. · In the v0.30 lane, controls VLLM_USE_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH. | With breakable on, prose S=4 varied 118/149/138/109 across identical runs; with it off, ~2% variation. · With 1, 10-18% variation across identical runs; with 0, prose 60.0/95.5/145.2/234.8 and code 76.5/140.2/248.0/441.2 tok/s (S=1/2/4/8). | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup, 43 Qwen recipe |
| `VLLM_ALLOW_LONG_MAX_MODEL_LEN` | 1 | Allows a max_model_len larger than the config's max_position_embeddings. | vLLM logs must be used with extreme caution; it stays set even with YaRN off (harmless, fable §4.6). | 08 Position & RoPE, 31 PagedAttention & KV |
| `VLLM_EXECUTE_MODEL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | dspark uses 1800 (not used here) | Execution timeout of a model step on the workers. | The inventory only records that the neighboring dspark image uses 1800; it is not used in this recipe. | 30 vLLM architecture |
| `VLLM_USE_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH` | ${V030_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH:-0} (v0.30 lane) · 0 in the recipe (v0.30 lane) | Turns breakable CUDA graphs on/off in vLLM 0.30, which turns them on by default for Qwen4Exp. · vLLM env var that turns on 'breakable' CUDA graphs. | On GB10 it caused 10–18% variance and −13–19% at S≥2. · Off in the recipe because turning it on caused 10-18% variation across identical runs. | 29 CUDA graphs & warmup |

#### KV cache

| Name | Value | What it does | Why it matters | Study in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--kv-cache-dtype` | fp8 (recipe) / auto | vLLM arg that sets the dtype of the main KV of the 12 QSA layers (+1 MTP). | Only works with the patched QSA backend. The gain is ×1.70 and not ×2 because the QSA indexer/compressor and the GDN/PLE states stay in BF16/fp32. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 31 PagedAttention & KV |
| `--kv-cache-memory` | not emitted (via EXTRA_VLLM_ARGS); 33651431445 (31.34 GiB), 41360080896 (38.52 GiB), 44208844800 (41.17 GiB, MTP-off), 45502283776 (42.38 GiB ≈ 3.0M tokens) | Pins the KV pool in bytes, ignoring the GMU for the KV. | vLLM suggests these values in the log at 0.835: ~6.5 GiB (~+740k fp8 tokens) went unused; it is the basis of the 1M + concurrency profile (seqs 3). | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 27 Unified memory |
| `--mamba-ssm-cache-dtype` | bfloat16 (optional) | vLLM arg that sets the dtype of the recurrent state (mamba/GDN layers) in the cache. | Overrides mamba_ssm_dtype=float32; the GDN state in fp32 takes 3 MiB per layer (1.5 MiB/GPU on TP2). | 14 Linear attention & hybrids, 23 Quantized KV cache |
| `block_size (1600 tokens)` | 1,600 tokens (matched to the GDN page); 1,664 with MTP 3 cited in the block-drop | Attention block size in the hybrid KV cache; vLLM equalizes the page sizes of attention and mamba state. | Prefix caching only matches at block boundaries, and the block drop discarded a whole block per turn. | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 33 Prefix caching |
| `bloco de atenção (block size)` | 1600 tokens (reported); 1664 at MTP 3 on single-Spark | KV page size chosen by vLLM, raised so that the attention page is ≥ the Mamba/GDN state page. | The prefix cache only hits at this block's boundaries (mamba align mode); the exact value depends on KV dtype × SSM dtype × TP. | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 33 Prefix caching |
| `k_scale_ptr / v_scale_ptr (layer._k_scale / layer._v_scale)` | 0-d fp32 per-tensor scales; 1.0 if None | FP8 KV scales passed to the kernel: scores *= k_scale after dot(q,k) and output *= v_scale at the end. | Applying them after the dot is algebraically equivalent and avoids materializing dequantized FP32 tiles. (inferred) Without calibrated scales in the checkpoint, vLLM uses 1.0. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 28 Kernels |
| `KV_BYTES_PER_TOKEN` | 29482 (bf16, measured; 28.8 KiB) | KV bytes per token used in the TP1 budget calculation. | Includes attention + GDN/indexer state; pure attention without MTP would be 24,576 B/token (≈84%). | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 10 Prefill, decode & KV |
| `KV_CACHE_DTYPE` | fp8 (default and fallback) / auto (=bf16); lane v0.30: auto, fp8 opt-in · fp8 (default) / auto (=bf16) / fp8_e4m3 | dtype of the main KV of the 12 QSA layers (+MTP), passed as --kv-cache-dtype; an fp8* value automatically applies patch_qsa_fp8_kv.py. · .env variable that becomes --kv-cache-dtype and decides whether the patch_qsa_fp8_kv.py patch is mounted. | 3,652,200 vs 2,131,159 tokens (1.70×/GiB); cost −5.3% average throughput (−1.4% prose, −9.2% code); tok/step drops because the sparse indexer picks different blocks, ms/step +11% at S=8. Do not pass the flag by hand: use the .env so the patch gets applied. · fp8: 3,652,200 vs 2,131,159 tokens in bf16 (×1.70) at the same GMU; measured cost | 23 Quantized KV cache, 43 Qwen recipe |
| `KV_CACHE_MEMORY` | empty (TP1); pin in bytes | Pins the KV pool in bytes on the TP1 lane (becomes --kv-cache-memory). | Alternative to the derived GMU when you want an exact KV pool (pinned in bytes) on the minimum-headroom lane. | 31 PagedAttention & KV, 27 Unified memory |
| `KV_MULT` | 0.5 if fp8 | KV bytes multiplier in the TP1 budget. | FP8 halves the attention part (an approximation; the real figure is 1.70×, not 2×). | 23 Quantized KV cache |
| `MAMBA_SSM_CACHE_DTYPE` | bfloat16 (sample) / empty = float32 from the checkpoint (start.sh fallback) · bfloat16 in .env.sample; empty = float32 from the checkpoint | dtype of the recurrent state of the 36 GDN layers (--mamba-ssm-cache-dtype); the fused GDN kernel accepts bf16 (FUSED_GDN_STATE_DTYPES). · .env variable that becomes --mamba-ssm-cache-dtype (dtype of the GDN state). | Halves the ~0.23 GB/sequence moved per step and the mamba page, which shrinks the attention block (single-Spark 3,200 → 1,664): +8.5% at 8 streams, +6.8% at 1, needles 15/15 at 32k. Not measured in isolation on dual; the empty fallback loses the optimization. · Half the state bytes per step and a smaller mamba page; not measured on dual. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 14 Linear attention & hybrids |
| `supported_kv_cache_dtypes` | stock ['auto','bfloat16'] → patch ['auto','bfloat16','fp8','fp8_e4m3'] | List of KV dtypes that the QSA backend advertises to vLLM. | Without widening the list, vLLM refuses: 'Qwen3.8-Flash-Next QSA requires a BF16 main KV cache'. | 23 Quantized KV cache, 30 vLLM architecture |
